市場焦點
聯準會9月
升息一碼
• 聯邦基準利率升至 3.75%~4.00%，經濟預測部分上調2026-2028年GDP成長率、失業率皆下
調，通膨降溫進程仍相對緩慢 ，點陣圖顯示今年還有升息一碼的機會 ，這次升息對股票而言 ，
聯準會沒有準備以大幅犧牲經濟與就業換取通膨下降 ，降低了硬著陸風險；對債券而言 ，短
端仍須消化更高的政策利率，但長端反而可能受惠於抗通膨可信度改善。
AI模型
開發放緩
• Anthropic、OpenAI及xAI相繼支持放緩最先進AI模型開發速度。不過，前沿模型開發速度放
緩，並不代表AI基礎建設需求同步轉弱。晶片、算力、資料中心及能源等AI基礎建設仍處於供
不應求格局 ，短期若因市場情緒或資金槓桿調整而出現回檔 ，較可能反映估值與持倉修正 ，

而非AI需求基本面反轉 。後續若前期累積的槓桿壓力逐步消化 ，且全球總體經濟及地緣政治
風險未進一步惡化，AI供應鏈仍具中長期配置價值。
台灣央行
• 利率決議重點： (1)台灣央行宣布基準利率維持不變 ，重貼現率、擔保放款融通利率和短期融
通利率分別維持2%、2.375%和4.25%。(2)放鬆房市信用管制 ，調整自然人第2戶購屋貸款最
高成數上限，由6成調升為7成。(3)上修今年經濟成長率及通膨率 ，GDP上修至11.48%、CPI
及核心CPI分別上修至2.03%及2.16。(4)央行預估明年AI基礎建設需求將持續貢獻台灣出口表
現，不過由於基期已墊高，預測明年台灣經濟成長率為5.82%。

資料來源：Fed，華爾街見聞，2026.9.17
貨幣決議與9月、7月聲明稿差異，不再強調能源衝擊 全員支持升息
• 聯準會9月升息一碼，聯邦基準利率在3.75%~4.00%。
聲明稿重點差異
紅色字體為9月的新增部分，括號內藍色字體為刪除的7月聲明措辭
FOMC以12票贊成升息一碼 批准發布以下聲明 ：委員會決定 ，將聯邦基金利率目標 區間上調至 3.75%至4%維持在
3.50%至3.75%，以支持聯準會的雙重使命。委員會延續其維持銀行體系充足準備金的政策。
儘管中東衝突在一定程度上帶來了高水準的不確定性 ，美國經濟活動持續以穩健步調擴張 。儘管地緣政治情勢 等因素
使經濟前景仍籠 罩高度不確定性，但國內支出保持韌性，生產力成長強勁，企業資本投資也相當強勁穩健。新增就業

人數大致跟上勞動力成長速度，失業率也變化不大，顯示就業市場目前仍大致維持穩定。
相較於委員會2%的目標，通貨膨脹率依然偏高 ，這在一定程度上反映了部分特定部門 (包括能源)因供給衝擊所導致的
價格上漲。今日的政策行動，將有助於讓通膨更及時地回到2%目標，委員會將實現價格穩定。

聯準會會後記者會問答重點整理
1
通膨「過高且持續太久」 當
前重心偏向物價穩定
通膨數據未改善，通膨過高且持續太久，聯準會當前的政策重心偏向雙重使命中的物價穩定 。透過政策
行動表明，聯準會對抑制通膨是認真的。
2
經濟、就業與地緣政治促成
一致升息
近幾週公布的數據證實美國經濟正在走強 ，基礎經濟成長率高於先前判斷；其次 ，勞動市場依然穩健；
第三，通膨持續高於目標，地緣政治局勢也改變經濟展望 。華許表示，這三項因素共同促成當天「堅定
且一致」的決定。
3
點狀圖暗示今年再升 1碼 但
不是政策承諾
最新經濟預測顯示，多數官員認為今年仍有進一步升息空間。12 名官員預期年底前再升息 1 碼，另有 4
人預期還會升息 2 碼，僅 2 人主張利率維持在目前水準。不過，點狀圖反映的是官員對適當利率路徑的

個別預測，並非正式的承諾。
4
拒絕提供前瞻指引 不預判下
次會議結果
面對下一次升息可能落在 10 月或 12 月的追問，華許表示「我不給出前瞻指引。」
他強調，先前公開表達的是對貨幣政策紀律的承諾 ，而不是對特定利率決策的承諾 。聯準會不會預判未
來任何會議結果，後續行動仍將依據最新經濟數據及展望作出決定。
5
升息非市場逼迫 迴避川普施
壓問題
面對此次升息是否由債市波動及市場壓力所推動的提問，華許予以否認。
他表示是根據就業走勢、經濟強度及整體情勢作出決定 ，市場價格可作為觀察依據，但不能決定聯準會
的政策。
資料來源：Fed，華爾街見聞，2026.9.17

市場反應與投資建議
9/16
市場反應
• 市場將華許記者會解讀為偏鷹，那斯達克指數、標普
500指數、道瓊工業指數皆下跌。
• 10 年 期 美 債 殖 利 率 在 利 率 決 議 公 布 後 一 度 降 至
4.9323%，隨後回升至接近 4.99%，在關鍵的5%關
口附近波動。
• 2年期殖利率則由盤中低點4.5975%升至4.7124%，
反映投資人重新提高對後續升息的預期。
• 黃金跌破4,260美元。
• 利率市場顯示 ，交易員加大 聯準會在2026 年底前進
一步升息的押注，12月升息機率升至約50%。
投資建議
• 股市：AI長期趨勢不變，但需留意利率對評價面的影
響，逢拉回就分批買進，相對看好美、台、韓相關供
應鏈
• 債市：預期美債利率 維持高位震盪格局 ，目前債市

配置仍以鎖利為主，且聚焦中短天期。
資料來源：芝商所，2026.9.17

台灣利率連10凍 明年經濟展望佳
• 利率決議重點： (1)台灣央行宣布基準利率維持不變 ，重貼現率、擔保放款融通利率和短期融通利率分別維持 2%、2.375%
和4.25%。(2)放鬆房市信用管制，調整自然人第2戶購屋貸款最高成數上限，由6成調升為7成。(3)上修今年經濟成長率及通
膨率，GDP上修至11.48%(六月預估9.45 %)、CPI及核心CPI分別上修至2.03%及2.16%(六月預估1.91%及1.90%)。
• 觀點：全球AI資本支出維持上調的趨勢，台股供應鏈訂單直接受惠，央行預估明年AI基礎建設需求將持續貢獻台灣出口表現，
不過由於基期已墊高，預測明年台灣經濟成長率為5.82%。
• 台股短線展望：上週隨著聯準會利率決議公布後，台股量能回升，顯示短線消息面或已利空出盡。權值股台積電領軍台灣加

權指數站上月線，技術指標偏正向。投資佈局方面，由於地緣政治、通膨及貿易關稅風險仍存在不確定性，建議AI概念持股
核心布局行業龍頭企業，留意中小型成長股較易受到外部因素而股價大幅波動。
資料來源：中央銀行，2026.9.17。f代表央行預測數。

美國零售強勁回升擴大內需支撐，惟價格壓力與升息提高市場波動
• 零售全面回升，消費動能展現廣度：美國8月零售銷售月增1.2%，創3月以來最大增幅，優於市場預期，並扭轉7月修正
後月減0.5%；非實體店面銷售月增 2.6%、電子及家電增 1.6%，餐飲服務亦增 1.2%，且扣除汽車與汽油後仍增 1.2%，
控制組更增1.4%，顯示消費回升並非僅由汽車與燃料支出帶動，第三季內需仍具支撐。
• 名目消費偏強，通膨與升息增添需求壓力： 不過8月零售銷售年增6.0%屬名目增幅，部分反映物價上升因素；同期 CPI
年增3.4%，汽油價格月增3.9%，能源成本上升可能壓縮家庭實質購買力 ，加上Fed於9月決議升息1碼，後續利率政策
仍將視通膨與經濟數據調整，使市場焦點由消費回升轉向評估高利率與高物價對後續需求動能的影響。

• 後市觀察：短期零售回升仍有助支撐美國景氣，但若能源成本持續走高，加上升息後借貸成本上升，消費動能能否維持
將成為重要觀察點；投資配置可偏向美國投資級短中天期債券 ，兼顧收益與利率風險，股票則可聚焦獲利穩定、具定價
能力的大型優質企業，降低需求放緩與資金成本升高的影響。
近三月美國零售銷售月率變化(%)

科技企業自由現金流可望由目前約2,450億增至2028年的6,330億美元，支撐亞洲科技供應鏈中長期發展。短期若因市場情

緒或資金槓桿調整而出現回檔，較可能反映估值與持倉修正，而非AI需求基本面反轉。後續若前期累積的槓桿壓力逐步消化，
且全球總體經濟及地緣政治風險未進一步惡化，AI供應鏈仍具中長期配置價值。

油價在美國中期選舉前，或將維持在高檔
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.14
伊朗戰爭期間卡達油氣收入暴跌
卡達政府的石油和天然氣收入 美伊衝突
• 美伊衝突短期內仍難見明確解方，彭博預估雙方後續可能採取的行動，主要可分為以下四種情境：
(1)伊朗承受經濟封鎖：伊朗承受美國經濟制裁與施壓，避免進一步重大軍事升級。在美方護航行動支持下，商船仍可維持通行
荷姆茲海峽，國際原油供應未出現明顯中斷，油價預估在每桶80美元區間波動。
(2)伊朗將攻擊範圍限於海灣地區油輪與美軍：伊朗主要鎖定海灣地區能源運輸船隻及美軍目標，美方則採取有限度軍事回應，

衝突維持在一定範圍內，油價預估在每桶90美元區間波動。
(3)伊朗擴大攻擊範圍：攻擊目標不再局限於海灣地區船隻及美軍基地，海灣能源基礎設施以及紅海等重要航運咽喉，均可能成
為目標。局勢升級期間，油價可能維持在每桶100美元以上，待衝突緩和後回落至70至80美元區間，此情境發生的可能性最高。
(4)美伊雙方全面擴大開戰：伊朗進一步擴大攻擊目標，導致美國軍事資產及區域能源基礎設施遭受重大破壞，甚至不排除以色
列重新加入戰局，油價可能升至每桶120美元。

全球債市拋售勢頭不墜，AI融資競逐資金推升美債殖利率
• 長端殖利率走高，財政與通膨壓力仍是主因 ：近期美國長
天期公債殖利率持續走高，其中10年期美債殖利率更突破
5%，創2007年以來高點；除能源價格上升推升通膨疑慮
外，美國財政赤字、政府債務及持續性的發債需求 ，也提
高市場對長天期公債供給與期限溢酬的要求 ，反映長端利
率壓力仍未完全紓解。
• AI企業擴大融資，增加公司債供給與資金競逐： AI資料中
心、晶片與電力基礎建設的資本支出快速增加 ，帶動大型
科技企業提高債券融資規模；截至 6月，華爾街市場策略
研究機構Yardeni Research在追蹤外國私人投資人過去一
年買進美國公司債約3,900億美元，高於美國公債約3,290
億美元，顯示資金配置確有轉向公司債的現象 ，不過市場

研究亦指出，AI企業發債對美債殖利率的直接推升效果相
對有限，應可視為推動長天期利率上行的眾多因素之一。
• 後市觀察：若財政融資與AI基礎建設支出持續維持高檔 ，
長天期公債仍面臨較大的供給與資金配置壓力；但 AI投資
仍具中長期成長動能 ，配置上可關注資料中心 、半導體及
電力基礎建設供應鏈 ，固定收益則以短中天期美債及高品
質投資級公司債為主，降低長天期利率波動的影響。

新興市場本幣債券擁高票息提供防禦力
• 高票息提供防禦力：對債券投資人而言，票息就像安全墊。當市場利率上升、債券價格下跌時，持續收到的票息可以部分甚
至完全抵銷價格損失。新興市場本幣政府債券今年的表現，比市場原先預期更具韌性。目前新興市場本幣債券的持有收益約
為4.54%，已經足以對利率風險形成相當程度的保護 ，即使今年存續期間對多數市場造成負面影響 ，票息收益仍讓整體報酬
維持正數。這也是為什麼新興市場本幣債券在美伊衝突爆發後一度重挫超過 6%，但之後仍能逐步反彈 。哥倫比亞、巴西、
匈牙利及墨西哥的債券表現尤其突出。
• 市場已反映相當程度的升息風險：問題在於未來幾個月投資環境可能比上半年更加困難，能源價格上漲推升通膨，也迫使新
興市場央行維持較為鷹派的貨幣政策 。市場目前甚至預期 ，19個主要新興市場中 ，有18個市場的債券殖利率可能在年底前

上升。殖利率上升意味著債券價格承壓，因此投資人不能只看表面的高票息，必須同時評估未來利率變化可能造成資本損失。
新興市場本幣債券各因子對報酬率貢獻
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.15
新興市場本幣債券票息的貢獻度

新興市場本幣債券目前最大風險在於利率與匯率
• 存續期間仍是今年最主要的拖累因素：一般而言，債券的存續期間越高，當殖利率上升時，債券價格下跌的幅度就可能越大。
今年19個符合基準指數條件的新興市場中 ，有14個市場的總報酬受到存續期間拖累 ，其中韓國、馬來西亞、印尼、泰國及
菲律賓受到的負面影響最為明顯。更值得注意的是，新興市場債券的利率表現與美國公債高度相關，因此，投資新興市場債
券，不能只觀察當地央行，也必須密切留意美國聯準會利率政策及美國公債殖利率走勢。
• 匯率可能成為下一個重要變數：新興市場本幣債券除了債券價格本身，還存在另一項重要風險：匯率。今年即期匯率變動反
而對新興市場本幣債券帶來正面貢獻 ，約增加57個基點。但如果能源價格持續上漲並形成滯脹環境 ，順周期貨幣可能面臨

更大的貶值壓力 。目前市場預期年底前新興市場貨幣整體仍偏弱 ，19種新興市場貨幣中 ，有11種預計兌美元貶值 。其中土
耳其、哥倫比亞及巴西貨幣的潛在跌幅相對較大。這代表即使債券本身提供不錯的票息，如果當地貨幣大幅貶值，換算成美
元或其他投資人本國貨幣後，仍可能侵蝕甚至吞噬債券收益。
• 小結與投資建議：目前新興市場本幣債券比較像是高票息換取較高波動的投資，而不是單純追求資本利得的交易。在殖利率
已處於相對高檔、但利率與匯率風險仍未完全解除的情況下，採取分散市場、控制部位、逢回布局的策略會比追高更為合適。

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.15

• 多重資產配置可有效風險管理：美伊衝突持續及聯準會利率政策，使今年金融市場震盪加劇，加上第四季美國期中選舉到來，
增加市場更多不確定性，不過從歷史經驗來看，地緣政治事件對股市的影響多屬短期現象，市場在初期情緒性下跌後，通常會
逐步回歸基本面，相較於預測事件發展，投資人更應透過多元資產配置分散風險，以提升投資組合的穩定性與長期抗震能力。
• 透過多重資產廣納收益來源：在利率維持相對高檔的環境下，投資人更應重視收益來源的多元化，從下圖可見，相較於過去幾
年，掩護性買權、非投資等級債，及投資等級債等資產的收益率均顯著提升，提供投資組合更具吸引力的現金流來源，其中掩
護性買權收益率已超過10%，非投資等級債，及投資等級債亦分別達7%及5%以上水準，透過結合不同收益型資產，不僅有助

提升整體收益潛力，更能降低對單一市場或利率變動的依賴，在市場波動加劇時提高投資組合的穩定度與韌性，兼顧收益與長
期成長目標。

7. 本資料所載部分基金涉及非投資等級債，投資人投資以非投資等級債券為訴求之基金不宜占其投資組合過高之比重 。基金經金管會核准，惟不表示絕無風險。由於
非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良
管理人之注意義務外不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
8. 基金年化配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除息日前一日之淨值×一年配息次數×100%。「年化配息率」為估算值。當月配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除

息日前一日之淨值×100%。
9. 基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動。投資人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績
效亦不代表未來績效之保證。本資料如以基金過去績效進行模擬投資組合之報酬率時，僅為歷史資料模擬投資組合之結果，不代表本投資組合之實際報酬率及未來
績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。
10. 部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之相關費用。基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部份，可能導致原始投資金額減損。各基金由本
金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至各投信基金公司及總代理人網站查詢。
11. 屬基金資訊觀測站ESG基金專區所列之基金，有關基金之ESG資訊，投資人應於申購前詳閱基金公開說明書或投資人須知所載之基金所有特色或目標等資訊。

長榮 為 全 球 貨 櫃 運 力 第七大 航
商，市占率 5.9% 
 長榮成立於 1968 年 9 月，實收資本額 216.5 億元。截至 3Q26，全球超過 1.3
萬位員工及 293 個代理行，航線擴及 101 個國家，總船隊數共 242 艘(自有船
比例超過 6 成)，預計至 2030 年仍有 72 艘船待交付。根據 Alphaliner 統計，
截至目前長榮為全球貨櫃運力第七大航商，市佔率約 5.9%。長榮 1H26 營收
結構為貨運收入 93%，船艙租收入 1.2%，其他 5.8%。

歐 美 航 線 運 價 優 於 預 期 ， 預 估
3Q26 稅後 EPS 19.62 元 
 長榮 Sep-26 營收 497.11 億元(+1.48%MoM，+63.75%Y oY)，3Q26 營收達成
率 105.5%，優於預期，主要受惠歐美航線運價走升，且旺季附加費及燃油附
加費挹注營收 。獲利方面 ，受惠運價上漲帶動獲利率提升 ， 惟中東衝突於Jul-26
一度趨緩後再度升溫，且考量燃油成本占整體營業成本逾 2 成，油價上漲仍對
成本形成壓力，預估 3Q26 毛利率 38.91%。由於營收規模擴大，費用率有望
下降 ，預估營益率 35.71%，假設 匯率 持穩 ，預估稅後淨利 424.74 億元
(+164.9%QoQ，+95.3%Y oY)，稅後 EPS 19.62 元。

歐美航線需求放緩 ，預估 4Q26
稅後 EPS 14.89 元 
 根據上海航交所 10/9 公告，上海出口集裝箱運價指數 (SCFI) 3,664.11 點
(+0.05%WoW，+215.76%Y oY)，隨中國十一長假結束，歐線運價短期反彈，
分別為遠東-歐洲線+3.41%WoW，遠東-地中海線+0.2%WoW；美線持平，遠
東-美西線+0.08%WoW，遠東-美東-0.04%WoW。觀察 10/11 公告跨大西洋、
歐 洲 及 跨 太 平 洋 航 線 運 力 分 別 變 動 -1.14%WoW 、 -1.88%WoW 及
+0.48%WoW。整體而言，隨歐美航線逐步進入傳統淡季，航商陸續調整運力
配置，其中歐洲線運力投放減少，美線則大致持平，顯示航商持續透過運力調
節支撐市場運價。

展望 4Q26，需求面而言，歐線隨傳統旺季結束，終端需求動能轉弱，預期後
續運價將呈緩步回落走勢。美線方面，旺季需求已近尾聲，且部分船班裝載率
已有下滑，儘管 Sep-26 川習會後美國調降部分消費品關稅，包括玩具、小型
家電及節慶裝飾品等，惟目前尚未出現明顯大型備貨潮。供給面而言，隨水情
陸續改善 ， 巴拿馬運河管理局預計自2026/10/15 起將每日允許通行量由 32 艘
回升至 33 艘，惟仍低於原先 36 艘水準。考量大型貨櫃船通行具一定優先性，
目前對船期營運影響相對有限，惟通行量尚未完全恢復，仍可能使美東線有效
運力有所收斂。另，美伊衝突及紅海通行風險仍存， Gemini 聯盟持續恢復部
分紅海航線，近期亦傳出 Premier Alliance 有望於 4Q26 恢復部分紅海航線，
若後續其他航商陸續跟進，繞航所吸收之有效運力恐逐步釋放，對市場運價形
成壓力。

整體而言，儘管近洋線進入傳統旺季，惟長榮歐美航線營收占比較高，預期整
體運價及貨量將隨淡季到來而回落 ， 預估4Q26 營收 1,350.87 億元(-8%QoQ，
+57.6%Y oY)。獲利方面，儘管油價仍維持高位，惟隨貨量減少，相關變動成
本亦有望下降，預估毛利率 37.9%。另考量長榮往年於 4Q 提列年終獎金費
用，預期費用率將季增，預估營益率 29.4%。假設匯率持穩，預估稅後淨利
323.53 億元(-23.8%QoQ，+279.8%Y oY)，4Q26 稅後 EPS 14.94 元。

2026 年有效運力供給受限 ， 預估
稅後 EPS 45.75 元 
 展望後市，根據 Clarksons Aug-26 預測，2026 年全球貨櫃供給年增 4.6%，需
求年增 3.8%，供給增幅仍高於需求，惟供需缺口較 2025 年逐步收斂。短期而
言，受到缺工及天候因素影響，部分港口作業效率下降，近期塞港現象增加，
有助吸收部分有效運力。展望 2027 年，在尚未考慮紅海全面復航之前提下，
Clarksons 預估全球貨櫃供給+8.8%Y oY，需求+3.5%YoY，供需缺口將再度擴
大；若紅海航線進一步恢復，繞航所吸收之有效運力亦將逐步釋放，供給壓力
恐進一步增加。需求方面，則須持續關注停戰協議進展、美國關稅政策變化及
通膨壓力對終端需求之影響。

展望 2026 年，長榮持續優化航線布局，強化東南亞、中東、非洲及中南美洲
等區域配置 ， 並透過長約布局降低市場運價波動影響，整體而言 ，元富預估 2026
年營收 4,735.4 億元(+24.9%Y oY)。獲利方面，預估毛利率 30.8%，營益率
25.4%，稅後淨利 991.6 億元(+44.6%Y oY)，預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 45.8 元。
展望 2027 年，隨新船陸續交付，全球貨櫃船隊運力持續增加，儘管塞港情況
仍可能限縮有效運力，惟考量整體供給成長仍高於需求，預期市場運價將逐步
回歸常態。整體而言，預估 2027 年營收 4,097.3 億元(-13.5%Y oY)，稅後淨利
482.4 億元(-51.4%Y oY)，稅後 EPS 22.28 元。

### Page 1

十月 15, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  Powered by the EFA Platform 1

動態更新 IC製造 ‧ 台灣

台積電 
(2330.TW /2330 TT)

多、更多、再更多的 N3 產能擴張 
重要訊息 
台積電 3Q26 財報表現符合預期，管理層預估 2026 年營收將年增 44%。 
評論及分析  
3Q26 符合預期，4Q26 展望強勁。3Q26 毛利率 66.4%，受 N2 量產爬坡稀
釋影響，較我們預估低 1.6 個百分點，但符合公司財測。受惠營收成長及營
業費用控管，營業利益及營益率均符合預期。台積電 4Q26 營收財測為 523
億至 543 億美元，以中間值計算季增 14%，較我們先前預估高出 7%，毛
利率及營益率則預期持平，主要受惠 HPC 需求強勁及 N2 量產爬坡。 
N3 持續擴產，並評估美國亞利桑那州以外的擴產機會 。隨 4Q26 展望轉
強，台積電將 2026 年美元營收成長預估由原先 40%以上上調至 44%，並
維持全年資本支出 600 億至 640 億美元。台積電過去鮮少將單一製程產能
擴充至超過原先規劃的目標( 約每月 15 萬片)。然而，除近期宣布的三座晶圓
廠(台南 Fab 18 、亞利桑那州二廠及熊本二廠) 外，台積電再宣布於台南新增
一座 N3 晶圓廠，預計於 1H28 量產。整體而言，我們預估 N3 產能將由
2026 年底的每月 15 萬至 16 萬片，在未來兩年進一步增加 8 萬片，增幅達
50%。我們認為此次擴產反映三項需求：(1)Agentic AI 帶動的 CPU 需求；
(2)HBM4e 客製化基底晶粒；以及(3)AI ASIC 。此外，台積電亦正評估在美國
亞利桑那州以外地區擴產的可能性，例如德州，但目前尚未做出最終決定。  
不會將任何商機拱手讓給競爭對手 。管理層多次強調不會讓競爭對手取得任
何商機，體現在 N3 產能的積極擴充。此外，儘管我們預期 Google(美 )委託
Intel(美 )生產的 Humufish 目前沒有備援計劃。然而，在必要情況下，台積電
將協助客戶確保產品如期交付， 暗示若 Intel 的 EMIB-T 製程進度落後，台積
電有望進一步提升市占率。部分投資人可能擔憂台積電導入 ASML High -NA
設備的時程較競爭對手晚兩年，進而影響其技術領先地位。但我們持不同看
法，採用最新設備並不必然代表技術領先。若 在 2030 年前台積電能在不投
資昂貴設備的情況下達到相近的製程效能，反而 有望取得相較同業更顯著的
成本優勢。 
投資建議  
儘管 3Q26 財報符合預期，且我們預估 4Q26 表現將更為強勁，但在市場預
期極高下，投資人仍可能對短期毛利率走勢有所疑慮。考量未來的成長動能
及穩健的執行能力，我們建議投資人趁股價回檔時逢低布局。我們僅微幅調
整獲利預估， 但隨評價基準遞延至 2028 年，我們 將目標價上調至 3,650
元，仍基於景氣上行週期平均的 20 倍本益比，維持「增加持股」評等 。 
投資風險  
全球景氣不確定性；積極擴產導致供給過剩。  
增加持股‧維持

### Page 2

台灣 台積電

十月 15, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 2

法說會重點數據 
 4Q26 財測： 營收預估為 523 億至 543 億美元，以中間值計算季增
14%；毛利率 65-67%，營益率 56.5- 58.5%。 
 2026 年展望：全年營收成長預估上調至年增 44%，維持資本支出 600
億至 640 億美元。  
 毛利率稀釋：海外擴產預計稀釋 2026 年毛利率 2 個百分點，未來幾年
稀釋幅度將擴大至 3-4 個百分點。  
 產能規劃：  
 N2 製程家族產能預計於 2027 年增加至兩倍以上。  
 台南新增 N3 晶圓廠預計於 1H27 量產，另一座 N3 晶圓廠則規劃於
1H28 量產。 
 亞利桑那州 N3 二廠已完成廠房建設，目前正進行設備搬入及安
裝，預計於 3Q27 量產，略早於原訂時程。  
 日本 N3 製程預計於 2028 年量產。 
 A14 製程已開始進行產能準備，預計於 2027 年如期試產，並於
2028 年量產。

圖 5：公司概況  圖 6：台積電各製程晶圓營收比重

持續有所增加。 2013 年的八吋約當產能超越一千六百萬片。

廠，外加正在興建的一座十二吋廠。台積電提供以下服務：晶
圓製造，晶圓探測，組裝和測試，光罩生產和設計服務。

十月 15, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 6

ESG 圖表定義  
項目 定義 說明 
能源使用 
直接與間接能源消耗總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司運營範圍內消耗之能源總量 
- 能源使用總量 = 直接能源消耗總量 + 間接能源消耗量 
- 總能源使用量包括購買的能源、生產的能源 
- 就公用事業而言，輸電/電網損耗為其業務活動之一部分，被視為總能源消耗，數據不包括
為滿足能源使用而生產的電力(公用事業為出售而生產) 
- 就公用事業而言，用於能源生產的煤、天然氣或核能等原料不屬於「總能源使用」項下

購買之再生能源 
Primary再生能源購買總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司各種來源之能源消耗量與購買的能源中屬於自然界可再生者(太陽能、風能、水能、生
物質能、地熱能)之量 
- 如無證據顯示再生能源由公司生產，則所報告的能源數據視為購買的再生能源

再生能源使用率 再生能源占總能源消耗量之比例  
CO2約當排放量 
直接 CO2與 CO2約當排放量(公噸) 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量(範圍 1排放量) 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳(CO2)、甲烷(CH4)、一氧化二氮(N2O)、氫氟碳化物(HFCS)、全氟化合物
(PFCS)、六氟化硫(SF6)、三氟化氮(NF3)

CO2約當排放量營收比 
直接 CO2與 CO2約當排放量(公噸)銷售(百萬元)比 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量(範圍 1排放量) 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳(CO2)、甲烷(CH4)、一氧化二氮(N2O)、氫氟碳化物(HFCS)、全氟化合物
(PFCS)、六氟化硫(SF6)、三氟化氮(NF3)

廢棄物總量 
廢棄物總量(公噸) 
- 總廢棄物 = 無害廢棄物 + 有害廢棄物 
- 僅考慮固體廢棄物，但如液體廢棄物以公噸為單位呈報，則會將其加入求得包含液體廢棄
物之總量 
- 對於採礦、石油與天然氣等行業，tailings、廢石、煤、飛灰等廢棄物亦考慮在內

廢棄物回收率 
公司呈報的廢棄物回收率 
- 廢棄物回收率 = 廢棄物回收量/總廢棄物*100 
- 廢棄物轉化為能源或經由廢棄物焚燒產生能源視為廢棄物回收 
- 經由堆肥回收的廢棄物視為回收之廢棄物

總取水量 
總取水量(立方公尺) 
- 由呈報組織直接或經水公司等中介機構從任何水源抽取的總水量 
- 水井、城鎮/公用事業/市政用水、河水、地表水等不同之水源均予以考慮

環保支出 
環保支出總金額 
- 所有用於環境保護，或防止、減少、控制環境因素、影響、危害的投資與支出，亦包括處
置、處理、衛生、清理支出

員工流動率 
員工流動率 
- 包括任何原因(自願或非自願)離開公司的員工，如辭職、退休、自然離職/死亡、醫療失
能、冗員、裁員、重組、解僱、裁減或定期合約到期 
- 員工流動率 = (離開之員工/平均員工人數)*100 
- 平均員工人數 = (本年末員工人數 + 去年末員工人數)/2 
- 本財年末員工數 = 上個財年末員工數 + 新員工數 - 離開之員工數

女性經理 
女性經理百分比 
- 女性經理占公司經理的百分比 
- 如有不同階層之百分比，如最高階、高階、中階、初階，則會考慮中階女性經理之百分比 
- 女性經理百分比 = 女性經理人數/經理總數*100

女性員工 
女性員工百分比 
- 女性員工占公司員工總數的百分比 
- 女性員工百分比 = 女性人數/員工總數*100

教育訓練總時數 
所有員工教育訓練總時數 
- 僅考慮員工教育訓練時數 
- 包括一般員工所有類型的教育訓練(如健康與安全、環境、急難事件救援、技能與職業發展) 
- 如果數據以天為單位，則乘以 8，係假設 1天 = 8小時工作

每位員工教育訓練時數 平均每年每位員工總教育訓練時數  
資料來源：Refinitiv，凱基

Earnings upward revision on better OPM, robust
AI ASIC revenue on track; maintain Buy with TP
at NT$6,800
Mediatek will host an analyst meeting on Oct 30; we expect an inline GM/OPM
(guidance mid-point of 46%/15%), yet we expect a strong c30% QoQ growth
outlook for 4Q26 thanks to AI ASIC contribution and better mix/pricing for
consumer products. On the back of solid operating leverage, we are also looking for

OPM upside. We are looking for upbeat 4Q26 guidance.
With AI ASIC starts contributing in 4Q26 and multiplying growth into next two
years, we expect OPM expansion for Mediatek on the back of its operational
leverage. We lift our earnings projection for 2026/2027 /28 by 9%/ 25%/9%. We
maintain our Buy with TP$6,800 (30x of 2027 /28 EPS, from 35x previously, mainly
on recent macro concerns and concerns on AI capex beyond 2028).

### Page 7

Mediatek (2454.TW)
21 October 2026 Citi Research
7
Mediatek
Company description
Mediatek (MTK) is a fabless IC company that designs SoC for smartphone,
smart TV, and digital media devices. It also develops chips for connectivity,
networking, smart home gadgets, and automotive sectors. It was founded in
1997, is listed on the Taiwan Stock Exchange, and is headquartered in Taiwan.
It has sales and research subsidiaries in mainland China, Singapore, India,

the US, Japan, Korea, Denmark, and England. It primarily sells its products in
China and other emerging-market countries.
Investment strategy
We rate Mediatek shares as Buy. We expect Mediatek to ride on 5G migration
in emerging markets and market share gain of its high-end Dimensity series in
China market as well. In particular, we see strong growth potential from its AI
ASIC, NTN-satellite business in the long term. We believe solid fundamentals

and strong operating leverage will support Mediatek’s margins outlook.
Valuation
Our NT$6,800 target price (rounded) for Mediatek is based on 30x P/E to
2027 /2028 EPS, which is 1-std above its five-year average forward P/E given
the promising outlook from its AI ASIC business starting from 2H26. We
believe our target PE is also justified by Mediatek's global and regional peers'
trading at an average of c32x PER. AI ASIC development with CSPs should

provide more upside from 2027 onwards. We also see Mediatek as well
prepared for 6G and 6G+NTN Satellite communication. Despite lukewarm
smartphone overall deamand, we expect Mediatek to benefit from 5G
migration in emerging markets and its Dimensity series should also gain
shares in flagship models. Non-smartphone business is also seeing
stabilizing TV and Io T demand with Mediatek outgrowing the overall market
on its better technology in WiFi 7 and connectivity.

Risks
Key downside risks that could prevent the shares from reaching our target
price include: 1) weaker-than-expected growth from AI ASIC business; 2)
more severe competition in the smartphone segment; 3) slower overall
demand related to macroeconomic risks, especially for smartphone demand;
and 4) further price hikes from foundries.
Prepared for Kevin Lu

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.
Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst

expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment
rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient

valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating
and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of

information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price
chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and

investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the
expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.

Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:
Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in

reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is
moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

AI Credit Crunch?

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
AI Credit Crunch?
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 22 Oct 2026
View in browser
Photo by Ragnar Beaverson / Unsplash

I. AI Jitters
Investors have worried that AI companies are spending too much on data
centers. These companies' once ample free cash flows have turned
negative, forcing them to borrow. Concerns have mounted that they are
driving up financing costs and won't generate sufficient returns to justify
their huge investments. Now, another concern is moving to the forefront:
Does enough capital even exist to finance everyone's ambitious AI
spending plans?

### Page 2

Meta's earnings announcement on Wednesday brought these concerns to
the fore. Despite revenues rising 26% y/y to $64.6 billion, the company
reported negative free cash flow of $2.1 billion. It raised the lower end of
its 2026 capital expenditure guidance to $135–$145 billion and announced
a new class of non-voting shares to facilitate future capital raising.
Investors interpreted the move as a sign that even hyperscalers may be
rushing to secure financing before competitors absorb the available

capital.
Indeed, the Meta report stated: "Recent significant capital raises from
peers like Alphabet and SpaceX, alongside anticipated fundraising from
Anthropic and OpenAI, have created an increasingly challenging
fundraising environment."
Several industry leaders have confirmed this concern. Broadcom CEO
Hock Tan recently warned that capital could become the biggest constraint
on AI development. BlackRock CEO Larry Fink said that competition for

capital was the fiercest he had witnessed in his career. On Thursday,
Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang warned that neocloud demand is "off the
charts" but capital is scarce.
As AI companies compete for capital, rising financing costs increase the
returns required to justify investments, amplifying the original worry about
whether all that spending will pay off. Today’s market action showed how
these concerns are playing out across stock and bond markets:
(1) AI stocks got a shellacking. Meta's shares plunged 7.0% today after

yesterday's announcement, leading a broad selloff in AI-related stocks.
Nvidia fell 4.4%, CoreWeave fell 10.1%, and Lam Research fell 11.8%.
SOXX dropped 6.8% (chart).

### Page 3

The Nasdaq dropped 2.6%, its worst day in four months (chart).
(2) Investors rotated. The market broadened away from technology
stocks today. The equal-weighted S&P 500 edged up 0.7%, outperforming
its market-cap-weighted counterpart, which fell 1.2% (chart).

### Page 4

MAGS fell 3.4% today, while XMAG edged down 0.5% (chart). The latter
is still outperforming the former so far this year: 14.3% versus 10.5%.
The Dow edged up 0.02%, while the Russell 2000 gained 0.11% (chart).
Rather than abandoning equities, investors rotated away from the AI trade
into other sectors.

### Page 5

(3) The bond market's reaction was a happy one. Treasury yields
declined across the yield curve, with the 2-year falling sharply to 4.64%
but remaining well above the effective federal funds rate (chart).
The 10-year Treasury yield fell below 5.00% for the first time in nearly a
month, returning to what we consider its old-normal range of 4.00%–
5.00% (chart). The Treasury market rally suggests that investors are
increasingly concerned that tightening access to capital could slow the AI

buildout, one of the economy's most important growth engines.

### Page 6

(4) Our take. We view market-driven tightening access to capital as
healthy for the AI boom, resulting in more efficient capital allocation and
reducing over-investment risks. Importantly, we expect the key players to
retain financing access. The five major hyperscalers (Amazon, Alphabet,
Meta, Microsoft, and Oracle) alone are projected to spend almost $780
billion in 2026. Over each of the following four years, from 2027 through
2030, that figure is expected to exceed $1 trillion annually. Their strong

cash flows, balance sheets, and market access should allow them to
continue investing in AI.
II. The Middle East Wild Card
Another development amplifying risk-off sentiment today was escalating
geopolitical tensions in the Middle East. The New York Times reported
that the US and Israel have developed plans for possible renewed strikes
against Iran following the November 3 midterm elections. Meanwhile,
intensifying Houthi attacks on Saudi infrastructure prompted the US to

order nonessential diplomatic personnel and their families to leave Saudi
Arabia, while Saudi Aramco evacuated staff from its Dhahran
headquarters.
The price of Brent crude oil jumped to $113.17 per barrel Thursday, while
West Texas Intermediate crude rose to $103.95 (chart). That sent XLE,
which we've recommended overweighting, to a new record high today.

### Page 7

Yet, Kpler data cited by The Wall Street Journal showed Middle Eastern oil
exports averaging more than 24 million barrels per day during the week
through October 18, exceeding prewar levels of 23 million. Prices remain
elevated partly because of higher shipping costs, but more importantly
because of a geopolitical risk premium, reflecting concerns that such flows
may not be sustained.
The bottom line is that the Middle East conflict remains a wild card.

However, we expect US consumer spending and business investment to
remain robust, while federal deficits of roughly 6% of GDP (during a solid
economic expansion) provide additional economic stimulus.
We expect the US economy will continue to pass the latest stress tests
originating in the AI ecosystem and in the Middle East.
III. Miscellaneous
We expect to see lower bull-bear ratios next week after today's drubbing
(chart). If they fall low enough, that might set the stage for a year-end rally.

Then again, there might be a better opportunity to buy the dip when and if
bombs start falling again in the Middle East.

### Page 8

Gold sold off on today's commotion even though interest rates fell (chart).
We are expecting that the retest of $4,000 per ounce might hold and be a
good entry point following this year's correction (chart).

## Document 195: AI从_朋友_变_敌人_：中金测算中国云厂商ROIC仅11.9%，对内需挤出或快于美国

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI从_朋友_变_敌人_：中金测算中国云厂商ROIC仅11.9%，对内需挤出或快于美国.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-27
Pages: 17
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

测算显⽰，中国头部云⼚商加权 ROIC 仅 11.9% ，远低于美国云⼚商 19.9% 。且⼆季度⾃由
现⾦流已全⾯转负，须依赖外部融资。中⾦认为，在资源分配⾮完全市场化的背景下， AI
对中国内需的挤出效应或早于美国显现 —— 今年消费疲弱已是佐证。美国可承受约 6% 利率
和 50% 成本涨幅，仍有缓冲空间；中国虽挤出更早，但⻓期战略⽬标使政策容忍度相对更
⾼。
摘要：
导读
7 ⽉以来，全球 AI 链条持续调整，背后除了不断⾛⾼的成本外，更重要的是市场担⼼ AI 能产⽣的
回报撑不住⼤规模的投资，例如看似市场是在担⼼ CoreWeave 的信⽤⻛险，背后可能是担⼼
Anthropic ARR 的放缓，所以市场要算 ROIC （回报）与 WACC （成本）的账，这与去年四季度
市场担⼼私募信贷的 “ 剧本 ” 如出⼀辙。
因此，决定后续 AI ⾛势的关键在于⼤模型的迭代和新业务模式的兑现（如今年⼀季度 Anthropic
业绩超预期），这也恰是提升 AI 回报的关键。
图表： CoreWeave 的 CDS 飙升（违约担忧）引发⼀轮抛售

为什么要做这样⼀个研究？因为中美 AI 都⾛到⼀个关键节点：⾃⼰的能⼒撑不起⾃⼰的野⼼了，
需要向外借钱了，这也是市场要 “ 算账 ” 、担⼼收不回成本，⼀直波动的本质。
当然，借钱不是问题，借钱却赚不到匹配回报才是问题。但AI 开始向全社会汲取资源的时候，挤
出效应就会慢慢显现。那什么时候是临界点，也就是政策和经济能容忍的底线。
作为 “ 朋友 ”， AI 的拉动效果主要体现在直接的投资出⼝，财富效应，提升效率和知识外溢三个⽅
⾯。扣掉 AI ，美股⼀半的盈利没了，中国⼀半的增⻓没了。
作为 “ 敌⼈ ”， AI 的挤出效应体现在资源品和⾦融资源的挤占、政策空间（美国的挤出是通过利
率，因为主要依赖私⼈融资；中国的挤出是要通过财政与信贷投放⽅向）、还有就业。中国挤占
可能更快，⽐如今年的内需。
那什么时候从 “ 朋友 ” 变成 “ 敌⼈ ” ？我们测算，美国整体能承受 6% 的利率和 50% 的价格上涨，还
有点空间。中国对内需挤出更早，但⾮量化和⻓期⽬标函数也使容忍度更⾼。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

资料来源： Bloomberg ，中⾦公司研究部
图表：截⾄⼆季度，美国云⼚商的 19.9% 的 ROIC 仍能覆盖 9.6% 的 WACC
资料来源： FactSet ， Bloomberg ，中⾦公司研究部
除了要 “ 努⼒ ” 证明⾃⾝回报⾜以覆盖不断膨胀的资本开⽀外，中美 AI 也都⾛到了另⼀个关键节
点：即⾃⼰的能⼒（⾃⼰赚的钱，经营性现⾦流）撑不起⾃⼰的野⼼（资本开⽀的投资需求）
了，需要向外部融资获取⽀持（⾃由现⾦流转负），⽽⽆法像此前那样只靠⾃⼰赚的钱来投资。
当然，借钱不是问题，借钱却赚不到匹配的回报才是问题。
但⽆论如何，当 AI 在中美都开始向全社会汲取资源的时候，另⼀个越来越重要的问题也逐渐浮出
⽔⾯： AI 是否会逐渐从拉动其他经济部⻔的 “ 朋友 ” ，逐渐⾛向其对⽴⾯，变成挤出传统需求的
“ 敌⼈ ” ？换⾔之， K 型分化的上半端能否继续对冲下半端，使整体增⻓依然向上？
中美其实都⾯临这个问题：近期美债利率的⾛⾼， CoreWeave ⻛险暴露，中国内需的疲弱，都

是这个问题的某种折射。
只不过，中美的传导和挤压路径不同、容忍度和临界值也各异。搞清楚这个 “ 临界值 ” ，也就搞清
楚了经济与政策能承受的边界，对我们判断中美的政策路径与选择投资⽅向都⼤有裨益。

### Page 3

⼀、 AI ⾛到了⼀个关键节点：开始从全社会汲取资源来⽀持其发展，中国
可能快于美国
今年⼆季度，美国 AI ⾛到了⼀个关键节点：即⽆法⽤⾃⼰赚的钱（经营性现⾦流）来⽀持越来
越膨胀的资本开⽀，因此需要对外融资（股权或债权）来弥补资⾦缺⼝，标志性的事件即是今
年⼆季度除微软和 Meta 外的主要云⼚商（ Hyperscaler ）⾃由现⾦流都转负（经营现⾦流 - 资本
开⽀）。
图表：美国云⼚商的资本开⽀逼近经营性现⾦流
资料来源： FactSet ，中⾦公司研究部
当然，并不是说借钱就是坏事，恰恰相反，借钱可以撬动更多资⾦，也可以提⾼ ROE（ ROE= 利
润率 * 周转率 * 杠杆），只有借钱却赚不回来相应的钱才是坏事。对云⼚商⽽⾔，截⾄⼆季度
19.9% 的 ROIC 完全可以覆盖 9.6% 的 WACC ，更不⽤说不到 5~7% 的企业债融资。
只不过，当 AI 开始向全社会汲取资源后，我们就不能只看 AI ⾃⼰的回报，还要考虑由此对传统
部⻔的挤出效应，加总到⼀起是什么效果，最终来影响政策⾛向与市场⽅向。

美国是如此，中国也是类似。⽽且，由于中国的 AI 投资 ROIC 更低（截⾄⼆季度，中国头部云⼚
商如腾讯、阿⾥巴巴及百度的加权 ROIC 约 11.9% ，低于美国云⼚商的 19.9% ），且⼆季度⾃由现
⾦流均已转负，需要更多外部资⾦⽀持。在⾮完全市场定价决定要素资源分配的环境下，对传
统需求的挤出可能更快。
图表：截⾄⼆季度，中国头部云⼚商的加权 ROIC 约 11.9% ，低于美国云⼚商

### Page 4

资料来源： FactSet ，中⾦公司研究部
图表：中国头部云⼚商⼆季度⾃由现⾦流均已转负
资料来源：  Bloomberg ，中⾦公司研究部
⼆、主要经济部⻔划分： AI 与其他部⻔ “ 五五开 ” ；美国 AI ⽣产多，中国 AI
基建多
界定清楚新技术的投资⽣产、应⽤和其他传统部⻔，是我们判断 AI 是传统需求的 “ 朋友 ” 还是 “ 敌
⼈ ” ，以及⼀轮技术扩张是局部繁荣，还是⼴泛的经济增⻓的前提。
技术⾰命的经济影响，不能只观察技术⽣产部⻔。技术⾰命对经济的影响既可能来⾃新技术本
⾝的投资与⽣产，也可能来⾃新技术应⽤带来的⽣产率改善和新供给创造，也有可能通过要素
价格影响到尚未有效采⽤新技术的部⻔。
不难理解，新技术对不同部⻔的影响存在明显差异。参考 Stiroh （ 2002 ）的思路，我们将经济体
划分为四个部⻔，更好体现技术⾰命的影响，具体来看：
1) AI ⽣产部⻔，主要涉及 AI ⼤模型⽣产环节，如电⼦计算机、软件、数据中⼼ / 云服务、 IT 服
务；

### Page 5

2) AI 基建部⻔，主要涉及 AI 数据中⼼的基础设施和能源环节，是 AI ⽣产直接拉动的部⻔，如电
⼒、建筑、建材、⾦属、机械和电⽓设备；
3) AI 应⽤部⻔，包括⾦融、专业服务、研发、医药、影视传媒等；
4) 其他部⻔，与 AI 投资和消费直接⽆关的部⻔，如⻝品饮料、地产、交运仓储、⽂娱等。
对于美国，截⾄ 2026 年上半年，相⽐ 2022 ， AI ⽣产部⻔占⽐上升 2.4ppt ⾄ 10.3% ； AI 应⽤和基
础设施部⻔基本持平，合计⽐例约 40% 。其他部⻔依然超过 50% ，不过相⽐ 2022 年底下降
1.4ppt 。简⾔之， AI 相关与不相关部⻔⼤体五五开。
对于中国， AI 相关与不相关部⻔⼤体也是五五开，不过 AI 相关部⻔的结构与美国差异明显。截
⾄ 2025 年底， AI ⽣产部⻔占⽐不到 5% ，仅有美国的⼀半； AI 应⽤部⻔份额与美国相差⽆⼏；不
过 AI 基建部⻔的份额接近 14% ，约为美国的 2 倍；其他部⻔占⽐约 50% ，和美国接近。
图表：美国 AI 相关与不相关部⻔⼤体五五开

资料来源： BEA ，中⾦公司研究部
图表：中国也⼤体是五五开，但结构与美国差异较⼤，基建⽐例更⾼
资料来源： Wind ，中⾦公司研究部

### Page 6

三、 AI 作为 “ 朋友 ” ，如何拉动其他部⻔？拉动投资出⼝、财富效应和提⾼
劳动⽣产率
作为传统需求的朋友，AI 的正外部性主要体现在直接通过投资拉动增⻓和出⼝、提升劳动⽣产
率、以及财富效应这三个⽅⾯。
► 直接通过投资和出⼝等拉动增⻓。这⼀点在过去⼏个季度尤为明显，中美都是如此。⼆季
度，美国实际 GDP 环⽐折年增⻓ 2.2% ，如果扣掉进⼝因素， AI ⼴义需求（ AI ⽣产投资和基建）
对美国增⻓贡献 28% ，拉动约 0.6ppt 。同期，中国实际 GDP 季调环⽐折年 4.77% ， AI 贡献 47% 。
对中国的拉动主要体现在出⼝上， AI 相关产品对净出⼝同⽐增⻓的贡献超过 60% ，净出⼝对
GDP 增⻓贡献 21% ，拉动 GDP 增⻓ 0.6ppt 。盈利也是如此， AI 相关板块贡献美股增⻓的 53% ，
贡献了 A 股和港股 19% 和 2.4% 。换⾔之，美国扣掉 AI ，⼀半的盈利没了；中国扣掉 AI ，⼀半的
增⻓没了。
图表：⼆季度，美国实际 GDP 环⽐折年增⻓ 2.2% ， AI 相关需求贡献 28% 增⻓

资料来源： Haver ，中⾦公司研究部
图表：⼆季度，中国实际 GDP 季调环⽐折年 4.77% ， AI 相关需求贡献 47% 增⻓
资料来源： Wind ，中⾦公司研究部

### Page 7

► 市场上涨带来的财富效应。AI ⾏情下的股市上涨会带来明显的财富效应，⽽财富效应⼜会提
振居⺠的消费预期。 Chodorow-Reich 等（ 2020 ）通过对本地⾮贸易部⻔（如零售、餐饮等直
接受本地需求驱动的服务业）的⼯资总额的实证检验显⽰：股票财富每增加 1 美元，居⺠每年将
增加约 3.2 美分的实际消费⽀出。这⼀点在美国体现的更明显，得益于美股更强的表现以及居⺠
部⻔更⼤的股市敞⼝（ 401K 等）。根据美联储的数据统计， 2Q26 美股总市值中个⼈等⾮机构投
资者持股占⽐超过 42% ，相⽐年初增加 1.4ppt ；居⺠的资产负债表中，含权类资产的占⽐ 47% ，
较年初增加 2ppt ；今年以来的可⽀配收⼊中，股息等收⼊占⽐超过 9% 。相⽐之下，中国的财富
效应仍有待提升。
图表：股市财富冲击后，⾮贸易部⻔⼯资总额上升
资料来源： Chodorow-Reich et al., (2020) ，中⾦公司研究部
图表： 2Q26 含权类资产在美国居⺠资产中占⽐接近 47%

资料来源： Bloomberg ，中⾦公司研究部
图表：今年以来， A 股 5500 家公司中位数回报 -17%

### Page 8

资料来源： Wind ，中⾦公司研究部
图表：截⾄ 2025 年，居⺠配置权益类资产的⽐例依然很低
资料来源： CHFS ，中⾦公司研究部
► 提升劳动⽣产率及知识外溢带来的正外部性。 AI 拉动经济的第⼀性原理来⾃其相对低成本、
⾼效率完成任务后对劳动⽣产率的提升。Anthropic 测算， AI 辅助可使部分任务完成时间平均缩
短 80% ，且相对劳动⼒成本更低。随着⼤模型应⽤场景⽇益丰富，美国⾮农部⻔整体劳动⽣产
率有所提升。其中， AI 投⼊较多的⾏业如信息技术、⾦融保险的劳动⽣产率改善尤为明显。⽣产
效率的改善，⼀⽅⾯提升了全要素⽣产率，另⼀⽅⾯，也驱动了词元调⽤量持续⾼速增⻓，云
⼚商算⼒投⼊不断加码， AI 算⼒、基础设施等相关环节需求快速扩张。
知识外溢带来正外部性：模型、软件、数据和组织经验向更多企业传播，可降低后续成本，使
此前 AI 使⽤强度较低的其他部⻔逐渐转变为应⽤部⻔。知识扩散还可能产⽣未被完全定价的正外
部性，令 AI 的社会收益⾼于私⼈收益。 2025 年， Brynjolfsson 等（ 2026 ）估计，美国⽤⼾从⽣

成式 AI 聊天⼯具获得的年度消费者剩余约为 1162 亿美元。同期 Gartner 预测全球终端购买者在⽣
成式 AI 模型上的⽀出仅为 142 亿美元，前者约为后者的 8.2 倍。对照其他⾏业， UberX ⽤⼾每⽀付
1 美元基础⻋费产⽣约 1.57 美元的消费者剩余，移动通信的消费者剩余为 1 倍，⽣成式 AI 则⾼达
8.2 倍。从结果看， AI 的社会收益显著⾼于私⼈收益，因此仅以模型市场交易额衡量其经济贡
献，将系统性遗漏掉最⼤的⼀块社会福利价值。

### Page 9

四、 AI 作为 “ 敌⼈ ” ，如何挤出其他部⻔？挤占要素资源、约束政策空间以
及替代部分就业
然⽽，⼀旦 AI 对社会产⽣的回报（⾃⾝ ROIC 与正外部性）⼩于 AI 对其它部⻔资源的挤占， AI 就
有可能从传统需求的 “ 朋友 ” 变成 “ 敌⼈ ”。 AI 开始需要外部融资和推⾼原材料价格，就是从要素
资源对传统需求挤占的典型例⼦，体现在三个⽅⾯：
► 挤占实物资源。 AI 消耗电⼒等关键资源，并推⾼其它部⻔关键要素的边际成本和产能利⽤
率。AI 资本开⽀并⾮单纯资⾦⽀出，还需要芯⽚、服务器、电⼒、电⽹接⼊、⼟地等实物投⼊。
当相关供给短期缺乏弹性时，新增 AI 项⽬可能通过推⾼电价、设备交付价格、建设成本及⾼技能
⼯资等形式，挤占其它部⻔的实物资源。 2022 年底以来，美国私营部⻔数据中⼼建造⽀出增⻓
近 5 倍。全球数据中⼼耗电量占产电⽐例从 2020 年的 1.0% 升⾄ 2025 年的 1.5% ，尤其是美国这⼀
⽐例从 2020 年的 2.6% 快速升⾄ 2025 年的 4.9% 。此外，过去⼏年随 AI 的发展，美国油⽓、电⽓

设备、采掘、机械和电⼦等⾏业产能利⽤率明显抬升。今年以来，存储价格上涨 110% ，推动美
国 PPI ⾥的电⼦元件及配件分项同⽐从去年底的 4% 最⾼升⾄ 6 ⽉的 29% ，为美国 PPI 同⽐抬升的
主要贡献之⼀。
图表：全球数据中⼼耗电量占产电量的⽐例不断抬升，尤其是美国
资料来源： IEA ， Ember （ 2026 ），中⾦公司研究部
► 挤占⾦融资源。 AI 跨越开始需要外部融资的节点后，对其他部⻔的挤占体现在价和量两个维
度： 1 ）价的维度，推⾼整体融资成本。AI 需求的强劲，叠加外部油价等供给扰动，共同推升了
成本。例如随着美国国债的上⾏， 30 年按揭利率已升⾄ 7.5% 的⾼位，⼩企业短期贷款利率也明
显升⾄ 8.2% 。从信⽤利差看，美国投资级企业债的信⽤利差并未相对科技⾏业明显⾛⾼，但⾼
收益债利差明显⾛阔。
图表：美国整体投资级企业债的信⽤利差相对科技⾏业并未明显⾛⾼

### Page 10

资料来源： Bloomberg ，中⾦公司研究部
图表：美国⾼收益债利差近期明显⾛阔
资料来源： Bloomberg ，中⾦公司研究部
2 ）量的维度， AI 巨量发债也会挤占包括美国国债和其他的融资需求，但⽬前也不显著。⼀⽅
⾯， AI 巨头增发⻓期投资级债会挤出⻓线基⾦对美国国债的配置需求，叠加美债市场三季度本⾝
的供给 “ ⼩⾼峰 ” ，共同导致了  8 ⽉的 “ 美债⻛暴 ” （《美债的 “ 问题 ” 出在哪？》）。另⼀⽅⾯， AI 发债对
⾮ AI 企业融资也会存在挤出作⽤，尤其是市场预期 2027 年美国科技⻰头信⽤债发⾏规模再新增
2000~3000 亿美元，只不过⽬前挤出作⽤还不显著。科技⾏业（信息技术 + 通信）在美国⾮⾦融
企业债供给中的⽐例已升⾄ 40~50% ，但⾮⾦融⾮科技发债并未明显减少。即便扣除到期后的净
供给，⾮⾦融⾮科技的投资级净供给也仍然维持正增。
图表：科技⾏业（信息技术 + 通信）在美国⾮⾦融企业债供给中的⽐例已升⾄ 40~50%

### Page 11

资料来源： Bloomberg ，中⾦公司研究部
图表：美国⾮⾦融⾮科技⾏业投资级企业债净供给仍维持正增
资料来源： Bloomberg ，中⾦公司研究部
► 约束政策空间。 AI 需求强劲也约束美联储的政策空间。美国经济也是 K 型分化，以地产为代表
的传统需求承受⾼利率压制，成屋销售 5 ⽉后再度转弱。按理说，美联储应该降息来提振传统需
求才对，但现在却不得不加息以应对更强的投资和更⾼的通胀。据美联储估算，美国⾃然利率
已从 2022 年底的 0.99% 升⾄ 1.44% 。年初⾄今， 10 年美债利率上⾏的 79bp 中，绝⼤部分由实际
利率（ 71bp ）⽽⾮通胀预期（ 9bp ）贡献。
图表：年初⾄今 10 年美债利率上⾏，⼤部分由实际利率（ 71bp ）⽽⾮通胀预期（ 9bp ）贡献

### Page 12

资料来源： Bloomberg ，中⾦公司研究部
相⽐美国，中国的挤占主要体现在财政与信贷资源上。⼀⽅⾯，财政资源更多向 “ 六张⽹ ” 建设等
新质⽣产⼒⽅向倾斜；另⼀⽅⾯，信贷资源的投放也体现了明显的政策导向，中国新增贷款向
科技等领域集中，房地产与消费相关贷款占⽐⼤幅下滑，今年以来消费仍然疲弱、居⺠资产负
债表收缩。
► 替代部分就业。 AI 在提⾼⽣产效率的同时，对就业也有替代作⽤，尤其是 AI 暴露度⾼的⾏
业。2022 年底 ChatGPT 问世以来，美国⾮农就业⼈数增⻓ 470 万⼈，但⾏业之间差距显著，教
育健康（ +278 万⼈）、政府部⻔（ +61 万⼈）和休闲酒店（ +44 万⼈）就业⼈数增加的同时，⽽
受 AI 冲击较明显的商业服务（ -37 万⼈）、信息业（ -30 万⼈）及⾦融活动（ -9 万⼈）等则减少较
多。此外，据达拉斯联储研究，⾼ AI 暴露度⾏业的⼯资增速更⾼，但年轻群体和经验丰富群体之
间的⼯资差异更⼤，这可能使年轻求职者⾯临更⼤的挑战。
图表：美国受 AI 冲击较⼤的商业服务、信息业和⾦融活动等就业⼈数减少较多

资料来源： Haver ，中⾦公司研究部
图表：美国科技产业和⾦融就业⼈数 2022 年底以来明显减少

### Page 13

资料来源： Haver ，中⾦公司研究部
五、 AI 何时由 “ 朋友 ” 转向 “ 敌⼈ ” ？中美的路径和临界点不同；美国利率
6% ，中国政策容忍度⾼
既然 AI 已经开始从社会汲取资源，那接下来的问题是，AI 何时会从 “ 朋友 ” 转向 “ 敌⼈ ” ？这本质
上是测算经济和政策能承受的代价，⽐如当资源都铺向科技时， K 型的上端能对冲下端多久？美
联储何时停⽌加息、甚⾄转向降息？中国政策何时更多关注更弱的内需？
在具体测算过程中，需要注意的是，中美由于 AI 资⾦来源和政策⽬标不同，决定了挤出路径和
临界点也有差异：1 ）美国 AI 融资绝⼤部分来⾃私⼈部⻔，因此主要通过利率和美联储政策来挤
出； 2 ）中国⾄少有三成来⾃政策相关资⾦，且银⾏信贷等也有政策导向，因此更多通过财政和
信贷资源的倾斜来挤出。此外，中国更关注⻓期战略，因此对短期的挤出的容忍度也更⾼。
► 美国能承受多⾼的利率？整体 6% 左右， AI 7% 以上，传统 4% 左右。美国 AI 和传统需求板块的
投资回报相对融资成本差异显著。五⼤云⼚商的 ROIC （ 20% ）仍远⾼于 WACC （ 9% ），⽽传统

需求如利率敏感的地产，抵押贷款利率（ 7.5% ）早已⾼于租⾦回报率（ 6.86% ）。美股内部 AI 和
⾮ AI 的差异同样很⼤，我们将标普 500 中信息技术与通讯服务⾏业加上可选消费⾏业中的亚⻢逊
作为美股 AI 的代表，其余 406 只成分股作为⾮ AI 的传统需求代表。截⾄ 2026 年三季度， AI 板块的
ROIC （ 35% 左右）与 WACC （ 10~15% ）差值⾼达 21% ，⽽⾮ AI 仅为 3% 。
图表：截⾄ 2026 年三季度，美股 AI 板块的 ROIC 与 WACC 差值⾼达 21% ，⽽⾮ AI 仅为 3%

### Page 14

资料来源： Bloomberg ， FactSet ，中⾦公司研究部
AI 可承受利率约 7~7.5% 。我们假设 ROIC 不变，若五⼤云⼚商的 ROIC 与 WACC 之差收窄⾄
2015-2023 年 AI 趋势兴起之前的平均⽔平 8ppt ，对应 WACC 约 11.9% 。综合考虑美债利率上⾏对
债券成本和股权成本的影响，假定 β 不变，倒算得到⽆⻛险利率约 7~7.5% 。
传统部⻔能承受利率 4% 左右。1 ）以地产为例，假设 30 年抵押贷款利率与近年租⾦回报率
（ 6.2% ）打平，再根据抵押贷款利率（拆分为 10 年美债利率、 MBS 利差和⼀⼆级利差）分项中
MBS 利差和⼀⼆级利差 2015 年以来的均值，推算对应 10 年美债利率约 4~4.2% 。 2 ）标普 500 中
“ ⾮ AI” 板块（除信息技术、通信服务和亚⻢逊），假定 ROIC 与 WACC 之差回到 2015 年以来均值
4.5% ，对应 WACC 9.7% ，回归得到对应 10 年美债利率为 4% 。

美国整体经济能承受利率约为 5.5~6% ，考虑： 1 ） AI 对美国 GDP 贡献约 30~50% ， 2 ）标普中 AI
与⾮ AI 市值权重约 “ 五五开 ” ，加权得到美国整体经济能承受的利率约为 5.5~6% 。
图表：假设 ROIC 不变，若云⼚商 ROIC 与 WACC 之差回到均值，对应的美债收益率 7~7.5%
资料来源： Bloomberg ， FactSet ，中⾦公司研究部
图表：若抵押贷款利率与近年租⾦回报率（ 6.2% ）打平，则对应 10 年美债利率在 4% 左右
资料来源： Bloomberg ， Haver ，中⾦公司研究部

### Page 15

► 美国能承受多⾼的成本上升？整体 50% ， AI 应⽤部⻔ 4-5% ，其他部⻔ 8-10% 。AI ⽣产投资⼀
⽅⾯拉动上游产业链的需求，但也带动原材料和相关商品价格上涨，挤压其他部⻔利润。我们
测算，假设云⼚商资本开⽀达到 9000 亿美元，剔除进⼝漏出效应，如果 AI 相关成本涨价 50% ，
那么资本开⽀带来的 1617 亿美元总盈利（ GOS ）将被成本涨价和进⼝漏出抵消；当成本涨价
3.6% ，那应⽤部⻔从资本开⽀获得的收益将被成本价格上涨挤出；若成本上涨 8.6% ，那其他部
⻔的收益也将被抹平。 AI ⽣产、基础设施部⻔在成本涨价过程中整体没有明显损失，甚⾄部分受
益。
图表： AI 主要成本项变动对盈利削弱的情景假设
资料来源： BEA ，中⾦公司研究部
► 中国政策能承受多⼤的挤出？由于政策更多关注⻓期战略⽬标，⽽⽆法简单量化测算，因此
需要从三个维度来 “ 测算 ” 政策的临界点：1 ）增⻓，《辅导百问》指出，为实现⻓期增⻓⽬标，
2035 年实现⼈均国内⽣产总值达到中等发达国家⽔平；2 ）就业，关乎社会稳定，⽬前尚未达

到 “924” 政策发⼒前低点；3 ）杠杆与资产负债表。任何低回报的投资最终都只能转化为债务和过
剩产能。不论是银⾏还是地⽅政府的债务，最终背后的信⽤都是中央政府。但狭义⼝径看，财
政空间相对有限（⼴义⾚字率已经⾼达 8% 以上，⾮⾦融部⻔整体的杠杆率 308% ，甚⾄⾼于美
国的 251% ）。
“ ⼗五五 ” 规划中将 “ 加快⾼⽔平科技⾃⽴⾃强，引领发展新质⽣产⼒ ” 进⾏专章部署，⾃主可控和
科技创新是关键两⼤中⻓期抓⼿。战略⽬标明确、短期压⼒可控、财政空间没那么⼤，因此财
政⼒度和政策⽅向的⼤幅转向可能还需要⼀定时间或更⼤压⼒。

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
• Central banks’ communication is carefully scrutinised for
every nuance. Lately, there seems to have been more RBA
communication to pore over. The method of delivery has
also evolved. This has sometimes caused confusion when
tone seems to vary. Different audiences call for different

tone and varying complexity.
• The content has evolved, too. RBA officials are increasingly
using speeches directed to professional audiences to
discuss more technical estimates of inferred variables such
as neutral rates and financial conditions. They are also
highlighting that monetary policy combats inflation in more
ways than just squeezing mortgage holders.
• One thing has not changed: the RBA is not in the business
of providing ‘forward guidance’ promises about the future

path of interest rates. It does, however, give more running
commentary about how it is interpreting recent data. These
interpretations are themselves useful signals about what
the RBA thinks it might do.
Market participants pore over every utterance by central banks
for clues to future rate moves. They will have plenty to pore over
in the run-up to the November RBA decision. After the flurry
of fireside chats this week, ’60 Minutes’ will run a pre-recorded

interview with chief economist Sarah Hunter on Sunday. Next
Wednesday, the whole senior policy team will appear before
Senate Estimates, just as the quarterly CPI is released. (This
timing will have been the Senate’s choice, not the RBA’s.) After
the decision will come the Statement on Monetary Policy, a
media release and press conference. More speeches will follow,
including one by the Deputy Governor on the Thursday.
RBA communication does seem to have changed, not just

following the RBA Review but also more recently. There may
be fewer policy meetings each year, but the communication
outside that certainly feels more frequent than a couple of years
ago. Back in 2024, speaking engagements felt a little sparse
outside the post-meeting media conference and parliamentary
appearances. We have counted 58 public engagements so
far this year on the Speeches page on the RBA’s website.
This compares with 58 for the whole of last year and 52 in

2024. Excluding the ‘compulsory’ press conferences and
parliamentary appearances, fewer than half of the 2024 events
were related to economics, markets and monetary policy.
The ‘how’ has changed
The way the RBA communicates with markets has changed
as well. There are fewer set-piece speeches on policy and a
Open mouth operations
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
lot more semi-scripted ‘fireside chats’. Compared with a few
years ago, there are more interviews across a wide range of

media. This is a big change from the pre-Review world. It was
a huge deal when the previous Governor appeared on the
7:30 Report ; it is hard to imagine him agreeing to an Assistant
Governor appearing on 60 Minutes.
Part of the change reflects increased efforts to reach a general
audience. Central banks want to explain why they do what
they do. This matters especially when interest rates are rising
but the household sector is not the source of the inflation.

Speaking differently to different audiences can sometimes
confuse markets. As the RBA’s Chief Communication Officer
highlighted in a recent panel discussion, the post-meeting
media conference is designed to speak to the public. Market
participants and economists certainly tune in, but we are not
the primary audience. So, if you think that what the Governor
said in the media conference sounded more ‘dovish’ than
what another official says in a more market-oriented forum,

it is partly because she is trying to convey that the RBA
understands that its recent policy actions are making things
harder for many Australians. But this is in much the same way
that a doctor might say, “This might hurt a bit.” It does, but that
will not stop the treatment.
A related issue is that it is easier to control tone in pre-drafted
material such as the minutes and set-piece speeches than in
unstructured Q&A. Senior central bankers are trained to be

careful with their words in unscripted settings. If the tone
differs, the minutes are still more likely to say what the Board
really means than an off-the-cuff answer.
Greater communication with the broader public might have
reduced the pressure to make the set-piece speeches more
accessible. Research on central bank communication often
uses measures such as the Flesch–Kincaid Grade Level and
Flesch Reading Ease score. A comprehensive analysis was

beyond the scope of this note. However, a spot comparison
suggests four speeches from 2026 ( 1, 2, 3, 4) were harder
to read than four from 2021 (1, 2, 3, 4). The 2021 speeches
scored in the 11 to 12 range, which is high-school-level
“The RBA does not want to promise what it will
do, but it is increasingly trying to tell us what
it thinks. "

### Page 3

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
complexity. The 2026 speeches scored between 12 and
15, which is academic or technically dense text requiring
university-level education. (Including two of my own 2021
speeches is an obvious caveat, but the two speeches by then-
Governor Phil Lowe also scored better on these metrics than

the sample from this year. It should be noted, though, that
readability metrics do not necessarily imply better clarity if the
concepts discussed are more abstract.)
The ‘what’ has changed
As noted above, the content of RBA communication has
evolved, too. As its intellectual framework has begun to put
more weight on the balance of supply and demand, and those
invisible ‘star’ variables (NAIRUs, neutral real rates, ‘financial
conditions’, etc.), the communication to professional audiences

has increasingly focused on those topics.
More recently, the RBA has also placed more emphasis on
the full range of transmission channels from interest rates to
inflation (though not, as we have noted previously, the labour
supply channel). This is understandable in a world of supply
shocks. People need to understand that monetary policy is not
simply an exercise in hitting mortgage borrowers. We doubt
they would be comforted, though, by the Deputy Governor’s

explanation this week that the ‘cashflow channel’ was the
smallest of the transmission channels from interest rates
to inflation, with the exchange rate much more prominent.
That implies a lot of pain for a small group, for little effect on
inflation. It raises the question of whether other policy tools
or institutional arrangements might get the same effect on
inflation through the other channels without hitting a minority
of households disproportionately.

We have also seen a move away from using catchphrases
to summarise the position. From ‘narrow path’ to ‘not
ruling anything in or out’ to ‘cautious and predictable’, once
entrenched, a phrase can outlast its usefulness.
One thing that has not changed is the way the minutes record
the presentations to the Board and the members’ discussion. The
minutes rarely convey much new information beyond the post-
meeting statements. Sometimes, though, the minutes reveal the

Board’s reaction to the staff’s analysis. The August 2024 minutes
were a good example: the discussion highlighted just how
sensitive the forecasts were to key judgements by the staff.
The ‘why’ has not changed
Despite these changes, the underlying objectives of the RBA’s
communication have not changed. It wants to explain – and
perhaps justify – its decisions. And it wants to describe how
it interprets incoming data, especially inflation, the labour

market and the balance of supply and demand.
The RBA, like any central bank, is not really in the business
of providing ‘forward guidance’ about future moves.
Circumstances change, so it can never guarantee a particular
future path for interest rates. Some academic literature argues
that promising to keep rates low for a long time (or high,
for that matter) independently amplifies the transmission
of monetary policy beyond the effect of current-period

interest rates alone, by shifting the short end of the yield
curve. Central banks including the RBA have certainly tried
jawboning in this way from time to time. The prevailing view,
though, is that the potential cost of reputational loss if things
do not turn out as predicted outweighs the benefits of a
slightly stronger transmission mechanism.
In this context, it is the interpretation of data that provides
the signal about future policy directions. This is why it was

noteworthy that the Governor still characterised the labour
market as ‘a little tight’ after the September meeting, even
though the Board’s post-meeting communication did not. We
have been noting for some time that the RBA staff have a
downbeat view of supply capacity. Together with the RBA’s
oft-stated concerns about inflation, this is why the Deputy
Governor’s ‘hawkishness’ did not surprise us.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: managing expectations
While annual retail sales growth improved in September,
rising from 0.4%yr to 1.1%yr, the current pace of growth
is only a quarter of the gain seen just a year ago and very
weak versus history. Consumer spending continues to be

constrained by weak confidence, particularly with respect to
employment prospects and wealth. On housing wealth, five
years into the property downturn, house prices continue to
decline month-on-month and investment remains in a deep
downturn, –20.6%ytd.
Total fixed asset investment has been detracting from
annual GDP growth since April owing to a lull in high-tech
investment, a lack of confidence in future demand amongst
domestically oriented businesses as well as property’s

malaise. For aggregate growth to hold up through 2027 and
2028 as the contribution from net exports moderates, broad-
based stimulus in scale will prove necessary. Expect recent
targeted support measures to be broadened and given much
greater scale in coming months.
Finally, in the UK, inflation accelerated from 3.1%yr to 3.5%yr
in September, exceeding both market expectations and the
Bank of England's July forecast. The surprise was largely
driven by energy, fuel prices rising 5.4%mth. However, core

inflation also edged up from 2.6%yr to 2.8%yr as services
inflation increased from 3.4%yr to 3.7%yr. While not an
immediate concern inflation, the labour market showing
resilience, the ILO unemployment rate falling from 4.9% to
4.7% in August, suggests demand risks for inflation will need
close monitoring as well as supply-side concerns.
Next week will see the focus once again return to the US as
the FOMC meet and US Q3 GDP is released. For both events,

the implications for risks to the outlook will be the focus. The
week after, the US mid-term elections are due.
With little domestic data of significance, participants’
attention focused on RBA Deputy Governor Hauser's fireside
chat, which saw an abrupt shift in market expectations of a
follow-up rate hike at the November meeting.
Hauser warned in his remarks that the current inflation outlook
was "materially worse" than the RBA's August forecasts.

Three upside inflation risks were characterised as having
"crystallised" ahead of the September meeting – the Middle
East, the AI boom, and domestic supply constraints. Hauser
noted the Middle East picture "looks worse" given oil prices
are "about a third higher than [the RBA] assumed in August”.
He then positioned AI and data centre developments among
an "extraordinary sequence of positive demand shocks around
the world", with "higher AI prices, tech prices feeding through

into CPI". Finally, on domestic capacity pressures, the Deputy
Governor noted that "inflation's been coming in at or a little
bit above expectations, despite an apparent easing in the
labour market". He went on to state that the staff are currently
assessing "whether [the RBA] need to change [its] judgement
on capacity pressures in the labour market again" – in other
words, revise up their assumption for the NAIRU.
Hauser's comments prompted a material reassessment of

market pricing for a follow-up November hike, from just a
20% chance at the end of last week to a 50/50 proposition
today ahead of next week's Q3 CPI. For that release, we are
forecasting a 1.3% (4.1%yr) lift in headline inflation and a
0.9 (3.5%yr) increase in trimmed mean inflation. Our preview
provides more detail behind the component mix for Q3 as
well as our upward revisions to the preliminary Q4 forecast.
Provided inflation prints broadly in line with our expectations,

the RBA is likely to hike by 25bp at the November meeting.
Across in New Zealand, consumer prices rose 0.7% in Q3
and annual inflation eased from 4.1%yr to 3.7%yr. Under
the surface of this ‘as expected’ read though, several closely
watched measures of core inflation took a step higher to now
be at or slightly above the top of the RBNZ’s target band. Our
NZ economics team consequently expect the RBNZ will raise
the OCR 25bps to 3.00% at its October policy review, while

retaining a 4.00% terminal OCR forecast for September 2027.
Earlier in the week, China's economy surprised  to the upside in
Q3, GDP growth accelerating from 0.9%qtr to 1.2%qtr, lifting
annual growth from 4.3%yr to 4.5%yr – keeping authorities on
track to achieve their 4.5–5.0% full-year growth objective.
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics
Ryan Wells, Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
RBNZ now expected to hike in October
We now expect the RBNZ will deliver a 25bp hike at its
policy review on 28 October. While September quarter
inflation was slightly below the RBNZ’s forecast, the
underlying detail was firm. More recently, increases in

fuel prices point to a material lift in inflation by year-end.
We expect further interest rate increases over the coming
months, with the OCR reaching a peak of 4% next year.
Softer headline inflation but firm core pressures
leave RBNZ on course to hike in October
We now expect the RBNZ to raise the Official Cash Rate
by 25bp at its October policy review (previously, we had
expected a pause at this meeting). This change in view reflects
significant upside risks to near-term inflation relative to the

RBNZ’s previous forecast.
While in line with our forecast, the September quarter inflation
figures were slightly softer than the RBNZ expected, with prices
rising 3.7% over the past year (compared with the RBNZ’s
forecast of 3.9%). However, the details were less reassuring.
Excluding volatile items such as food and fuel, underlying
inflation pressures remained firm. Closely watched measures
of core inflation moved higher and now sit close to, or a little

above, the top of the RBNZ’s target band. We also saw an
increase in the number of goods recording price rises, taking
that measure back to levels seen in the wake of the pandemic.
Alongside the existing firmness in underlying inflation, the
risks for inflation over the coming quarters are firmly tilted to
the upside. That near-term upside risk has also been noted by
the Governor. Following a sharp rise in global fuel costs, we
expect inflation to reach 4.3% by the end of this year (above

the RBNZ’s September MPS forecast for +3.9%). While that is
mainly due to higher prices at the pump, businesses in sectors
such as construction are also reporting related increases in
transport and materials costs. Consistent with those trends,
we expect core inflation to remain in the upper part of the
RBNZ’s target band through 2027.
Reinforcing the case for an OCR hike at the October meeting,
the New Zealand dollar is notably weaker than the RBNZ

assumed: on a trade-weighted basis, the NZD is currently
3.6% below the RBNZ’s September projections. If sustained,
that would add to inflation pressures over the next few
quarters. We suspect this will not have shifted the RBNZ’s
medium-term outlook for inflation.
Several other developments need to be weighed against that
firmer inflation outlook. First, wholesale long-term interest rates
have risen markedly, and there has also been a more moderate

rise in some mortgage rates. The RBNZ is likely to note the role of
those increases in managing medium-term inflation risks.
In addition, the RBNZ is likely to continue to emphasise that
there is little evidence of a lift in businesses’ longer-term plans
for price setting at this stage in response to the rise in energy
prices (sometimes referred to as “second-round inflation
effects”). We expect the RBNZ to highlight the risk that more
persistent inflation pressures could emerge, depending on the

duration of the oil price shock.
We also expect the RBNZ to note that the labour market
remains cyclically weak and is not a significant driver of inflation.
Employment growth that remains weak relative to labour force
growth, subdued growth in job vacancies and low labour market
turnover are consistent with this view and could be emphasised.
Finally, the RBNZ is likely to acknowledge that while the
global growth outlook remains solid, there are significant

downside risks from the Iran war and concerns about the
sustainability of the AI investment cycle.
Balancing those concerns, we think the MPC will lift the
cash rate next week. However, a vote seems very possible as
differences in view will likely remain among the MPC about
how much the medium-term inflation outlook has really
shifted in recent weeks.
In September, the bulk of the MPC saw upside risks to the
inflation outlook while two members (Conway and Hansen)

thought the risks were balanced. Recent developments will
not have made those seeing upside risks to inflation more
Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
comfortable, while the more dovish MPC members will
likely focus on the ongoing downside risks for growth given
continued softness in house prices and the mounting pressure
on real incomes. Indeed, all MPC members saw some degree
of downside risks to growth which likely remain relevant. All

these messages should remain in the RBNZ’s updated views.
The MPC is likely to continue to present its decision as a
gradual evolution of the approach taken since July: a higher
OCR now provides insurance against the need for a much
higher OCR later if upside risks to medium-term inflation
materialise. We doubt the aim will be to push market interest
rates materially higher.
RBNZ to leave door open for further hikes, OCR
still expected to peak at 4% in 2027

In its forward guidance, the RBNZ is likely to leave open the option
of raising the OCR further at the December Monetary Policy
Statement, without presenting an increase as certain. While the
RBNZ will not publish an updated OCR projection at this review,
the press statement is likely to note that further increases may
be needed, depending on economic conditions. The September
projection showed the OCR eventually rising to 3.25%.
We expect the RBNZ will end up delivering another 25bp

hike in December, followed by a further hike in February.
That would take the OCR to 3.5%, roughly where the RBNZ
estimates the neutral rate to be. At that point, we expect the
RBNZ to slow the pace of rate increases (previously, we had
expected that point to be reached a little later).
We continue to expect further 25bp hikes at the June and
September 2027 meetings, taking the OCR to a peak of 4.0%.
While oil prices are generating significant swings in inflation,

underlying pressures remain firm. More restrictive conditions are
likely to be needed to bring core inflation sustainably back to 2%.
Scenarios
Our central scenario, with a probability of around 60%, is that
the RBNZ increases the OCR by 25bp and signals that a further
increase in December remains an option, without committing to
a markedly tighter future stance than it outlined in September.
Alternative scenarios include:
• A hawkish scenario (around a 20% probability) would see

the OCR increase to 3%, with the RBNZ placing greater
emphasis on upside risks to core and medium-term inflation.
That would suggest an upward revision to the RBNZ’s
projected OCR path in December. Downside risks to the
exchange rate could also be emphasised.
• A dovish scenario (around a 20% probability) would
see the RBNZ leave the OCR unchanged and signal that
the medium-term inflation outlook has not changed
meaningfully from its September forecast, despite higher

near-term inflation. In this scenario, the RBNZ would
reiterate its previous forecast: a 25bp increase in December
and one further hike during 2027.
How might the election play into the decision?
While next week’s meeting is close to the General Election
on 7 November, the decision taken by the MPC will not be
politically motivated. However, the election outcome is close.
And should it result in a Labour Party-led coalition, it seems
likely the new government would adopt the Labour Party’s

promise to restore the RBNZ’s dual mandate. That would
require the MPC to weigh the degree of labour market slack
more highly in its deliberations. As the degree of labour market
slack currently seems high, a new mandate might justify the
MPC taking more time to return inflation to the middle of the
target range (our earlier note discusses this issue). This could
figure into the deliberations of some MPC members if the
decision between leaving the OCR unchanged and raising it

now is viewed as finely balanced.

Private sector credit growth came in slightly firmer than

We expect the RBNZ to raise the OCR by 25bps to 3% at

outlook for year-end inflation and a stronger GDP starting

NZ: Oct ANZ Business Confidence (index)
Oct 29, Last: 51.9
Firms remained optimistic in the September survey of business
confidence, with both the activity and inflation measures
broadly holding steady. However, it’s likely that most responses
were submitted in the early part of the month, while fuel prices
took a sharp step higher from the second half of September. The

survey comes too late for the RBNZ’s October policy review, but
evidence about the breadth of cost and price pressures could
have a bearing on the RBNZ’s thinking about the total amount of
policy tightening needed in the months ahead.
NZ: Sep Monthly Employment Indicator (%mth)
Oct 28, Last: 0.2, Westpac f/c: 0.3

the Iran conflict and the consequent surge in fuel prices. The

September – we’ve pencilled in a 0.3% increase. Given the
history of this measure, however, both this month and the
previous month are likely to be revised down a little from their

Oct FOMC meeting
Oct 27-28, Last: 3.875%, Mkt f/c: 3.875%, WBC f/c: 3.875%
Core CPI inflation and producer price inflation both surprised
to the upside in September, and the price of Brent oil has
risen back to around USD110. Underlying momentum in
the economy also remains robust, although employment

disappointed in the latest update. Given the concerns shown
by Committee members over price risks and their collective
resolve to return annual inflation to target, the above data
could easily justify a follow-up hike at the October meeting.
That said, Committee members who have spoken recently
have shown patience with respect to assessing the economy
and positioing policy. The market has responded, effectively
pricing out the chance of an October hike in favour of a

December move, which is also our expectation.
US Q3 GDP
Oct 29, %ann’d, Last: 2.2%, Mkt f/c: 3.0%, WBC f/c: 3.1%
The US economy continues to grow at a pace well above trend.
This outperformance is almost entirely due to the strength of
investment in data centres and other AI related assets. The
pipeline for this activity remains strong, limiting near-term
risks to the downside.
Abstracting from AI investment’s contribution, other business
investment and residential investment is weak, weighed down

by high term interest rates and uncertainty related to trade
policy. Household consumption growth is volatile month-to-
month, but the trend is solid. Absent a marked deterioration
in the labour market, which is not our expectation, this
momentum is likely to persist, as wealth continues to grow and
household income gains neutralises inflation. Come 2027 and
2028, growth is expected to slow to trend, but not below.

Điểm nhấn thị trường 
Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -2.62 điểm tương ứng -0.15%, với thanh khoản cải 
thiện. Dệt may, Công nghệ và Máy móc là các nhóm ngành có mức giảm rõ rệt nhất trong 
tuần. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị -13,987 tỷ 
đồng, tập trung ở các mã HDB, PNJ và VJC; mặt khác, tự doanh đảo chiều bán ròng ở mức 
-30 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại đảo chiều mua ròng 1,267 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần 
qua nhưng đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -2,363 hợp đồng. Tự doanh mua ròng 3,726 hợp 
đồng nhưng đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -2,702 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam trải qua một tuần đi ngang giằng co, giảm nhẹ và đóng cửa ở 1.735 điểm 
với thanh khoản ở mức thấp, và nhà đầu tư nước ngoài bán ròng cả 5 phiên trong tuần. 
Thông tin vĩ mô tích cực với tăng trưởng GDP quý 3 đạt mức gần 10% là điểm sáng trong 
tuần qua, tuy đã không đủ nâng đỡ thị trường chung. Thay vào đó, tâm lý thị trường thận 
trọng trước số liệu lạm phát ở mức cao, đợt tăng giá xăng dầu trong nước và mặt bằng lãi 
suất trong nước chưa có dấu hiệu hạ nhiệt. Diễn biến tăng của giá dầu thế giới, và lợi suất 
trái phiếu Chính phủ Mỹ cũng là các yếu tố gây áp lực lên thị trường chung. Tâm lý thận 
trọng còn thể hiện qua áp lực bán giải chấp ở một số cổ phiếu riêng lẻ như PNJ, SHB..., dù 
một vài nhóm trụ như dầu khí, tiêu dùng có thời điểm đóng vai trò nâng đỡ chỉ số.

Trần Đức Anh 
Giám đốc Kinh tế Vĩ mô & Chiến lược thị trường 
anhtd@kbsec.com.vn 
07/10/2026

Triển vọng TTCK 4Q2026 
Tích lũy trong vùng nhiễu động

Chúng tôi dự báo vùng điểm hợp lý của chỉ số VNIndex thời điểm cuối năm 2026 
xuống 1,800 đi ểm, giảm mạnh từ mức 1,950 đi ểm đưa ra trong báo cáo gần nhất. 
Vùng điểm này tương ứng:

- Tăng trưởng EPS 2026 so với cùng kỳ của thị trường chung ở mức 33% 
(tăng mạnh so với mức 25% của báo cáo gần nhất), chủ yếu nhờ kỳ vọng 
vào các chính sách thúc đẩy kinh tế của Chính phủ, và lợi nhuận tăng 
trưởng đột biến ở VIC (xem thêm phần Triển vọng hoạt động kinh doanh).

- Mức P/E hợp lý của chỉ số VNIndex thời điểm cuối năm ở mức 13.2 (giảm 
nhẹ so với mức 13.5 thời điểm hiện tại - phản ánh quan điểm các yếu tố 
rủi ro sẽ tiếp tục duy trì). Đây là mức định giá thấp so với bình quân 5 năm 
gần nhất ở 15.1. Nếu loại bỏ yếu tố đột biến ở nhóm Vingroup, chỉ số P/E 
điều chỉnh hiện đã rơi xuống 10.9, thấp hơn các đáy gần nhất năm 2020, 
2022 và 2025. Trong bối cảnh kinh tế Việt Nam duy trì tốc độ tăng trưởng 
cao, lợi nhuận các doanh nghiệp niêm yết liên tục bứt phá, vùng định giá 
này mở ra cơ hội đầu tư trung/dài hạn. Chúng tôi cho rằng ngay khi các rủi 
ro vĩ mô quốc tế (xoay quanh vấn đề Iran), và trong nước (xoay quanh vấn 
đề lãi suất) hạ nhiệt, thị trường chung sẽ có cơ hội tái định giá lên mức cao 
hơn đáng kể so với thời điểm hiện tại. Dù vậy, khác với báo cáo gần nhất 
vào tháng 7, ở báo cáo này, chúng tôi đẩy lui thời điểm các yếu tố rủi ro vĩ 
mô hạ nhiệt sang năm 2027, đồng nghĩa với quan điểm thận trọng hơn về 
thị trường cho quý 4 2026.

Cụ thể, đối với vấn đề lãi suất, áp lực lạm phát lớn dần, đi kèm nhu cầu vốn cao phục 
vụ hoạt động đầu tư cơ sở hạ tầng sẽ khiến mặt bằng lãi suất duy trì ở mức cao cho 
phần còn lại của năm 2026 (dù thanh khoản hệ thống ghi nhận sự cải thiện đáng kể 
trong 1 quý gần đây). Đối với vấn đề xung đột Trung Đông, các cuộc đàm phán vẫn 
đang bế tắc và ít có kh ả năng dòng ch ảy dầu mỏ tại khu vực này được nối lại ngay 
trong năm nay.

Chủ đề đầu tư chúng tôi l ựa chọn cho 4Q2026 là đ ầu tư công, chính sách ti ền tệ hỗ 
trợ, và xung đột Iran.

Xem đầy đủ tại đây.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX hình thành mẫu nến Doji trên khung đồ thị tuần sau hai tuần giảm điểm liên tiếp, 
cho thấy áp lực bán đã phần nào được kiểm soát. Trên khung ngày, nỗ lực hồi phục đầu 
tuần gặp lực cản tại đường SMA50 khiến VNINDEX quay đầu giảm điểm, tiếp tục chớm phá 
đáy ngắn hạn; tuy nhiên vùng hỗ trợ gần đã tạo hiệu ứng thu hút lực cầu bắt đáy, giúp chỉ 
số thu hẹp đà giảm trong phiên cuối tuần. Sau nhịp điều chỉnh khá tiêu cực từ vùng đỉnh 
đầu tháng 9, VNINDEX đang bước vào giai đoạn tìm kiếm điểm cân bằng trở lại và diễn biến 
giằng co điều chỉnh có thể còn kéo dài thêm trong các phiên kế tiếp. Tuy nhiên khi xu 
hướng tăng ngắn hạn tính từ vùng đáy cuối tháng 7 vẫn đang được bảo lưu, chỉ số được kỳ 
vọng sẽ có cơ hội hồi phục trở lại tại điểm đỡ gần quanh 1710 (đây cũng là chốt chặn cần 
giữ để bảo vệ được xu hướng tăng ngắn hạn).

◼ 
 Sovereign risk
 : 
the government debt burden 
 is moderate and 
 will remain

deteriorate
 . The fiscal deficit could exceed 3% of GDP in 2026 due to support measures in response to the energy shock (estimated at 
less than 1% of GDP in H1 2026), but this will not endanger public
 -
 debt dynamics. Government debt should fall again in 2027 thanks 
to lower deficits and fast GDP growth. Debt was only 30% of GDP in 2025 (vs. 41% in 2019) and interest payments consumed less  
than 5% of fiscal revenue (vs. 7.2% in 2019). The government cover
 s its funding needs mostly in the local bond market, which is 
deepening gradually and not 
 highly 
 exposed to foreign investors. Government debt in foreign currency declined to about 25% of total 
debt in 2025 (vs. 38% in 2019); this is still substantial, but almost all this FC debt 
 is made up  
 of concessional loans from official 
creditors. On the negative front, high credit risks in the state corporate and bank sectors 
 are 
 very large contingent risks for the 
sovereign. Moreover, public investment needs related to climate change and infrastructure development will increase in the 
 medium 
term
.

◼ 
 Systemic credit risk
 : 
Domestic credit to the private sector is too large and still rising (141% of GDP in 2025 vs. 108% in 2019) while 
the banking sector remains poorly capitali
 s
ed. Credit risks and resource misallocation have long been exacerbated by opacity and 
supervision flaws, as well as the use of high credit growth targets to support economic growth (+15% in 2026). In 2022
 –
2023, banks 
faced severe funding stress and asset
 -
 quality deterioration resulting from the property and bond market crisis. The situation has 
recovered gradually since then. In the 
 short term
 , credit risks may increase amid higher energy costs and if  
there is  
a correction in 
the tech cycle. Some banks, especially small private ones, also remain exposed to VND funding risk (whereas banks do not rely  
much 
on 
 foreign
-
 currency 
 financing). Positively, reforms, including stricter capital
 -
 adequacy requirements, continue to be introduced 
gradually 
 in order 
 to improve banks’ creditworthiness (
 the 
 SBV plans to test the removal of credit quotas for the soundest banks 
from 2026).

## Document 273: Geopolitical turbulence complicates airlines’ flight paths for 2027

Source PDF: all report pdf/Geopolitical turbulence complicates airlines’ flight paths for 2027.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-27
Pages: 18
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Global air travel growth is set to rebound to 5% in 2027, but
airlines face tougher conditions. Demand remains resilient, but
the fuel price shock will spill over into 2027, while new aircraft
supply remains constrained. This creates divergent fortunes
across airlines, segments and regions
Rico Luman
The aviation industry now faces a growing challenge to deliver proﬁtable
growth as capacity remains constrained, costs rise, and geopolitical risks
intensify

The fuel-price shock will continue into 2027. Jet fuel
could account for close to 35% of airlines’ operating
costs in 2026, up from 27% in 2025. Tight reﬁnery
capacity and low inventories are likely to keep prices
elevated even if oil prices ease.
European air fares will have further to rise. Hedging
initially protected many European airlines, but that cover
will progressively expire during 2027. US fares have

already adjusted more quickly because American
carriers tend to hedge less.
Budget airlines are particularly exposed. Fuel represents
a larger share of their costs, while price-sensitive
passengers make it harder to pass on the full increase.
Premium carriers have more scope to raise fares without
signiﬁcantly weakening demand.
Ticket prices are unlikely to return to early-2026 levels.
Even if fuel becomes cheaper, resilient demand, limited
aircraft availability and wider inﬂationary pressures

should keep fares relatively high.
The Middle East disruption has reshaped global traﬃc
ﬂows. Traﬃc in the region halved in March and April 2026
and was still 15% lower year-on-year in August,
knocking an estimated 2-3 percentage points oﬀ global
passenger growth. This will also still impact 2027.
European airlines have beneﬁted from some traﬃc
shifting away from Middle Eastern hubs.
Aircraft shortages will continue to constrain capacity.
Airbus and Boeing had a combined backlog of around

15,000 aircraft by mid-2026. Scarcity is keeping aircraft
values and leasing rates high and forcing airlines to
operate older, less fuel-eﬃcient planes for longer.
Asia is becoming the industry’s main long-term growth
engine, and India in particular stands out. Asia’s share
of the global aircraft ﬂeet is expected to rise from roughly
one-third to around 45%, with India standing out: its
ﬂeet could grow by approximately 8% a year over the
next two decades.

Cost shock from Middle East disruption
most impactful
Air traﬃc to and from the Middle East has taken a signiﬁcant hit as

a result of the war, which has led to airspace closures and
passengers shifting to other destinations due to safety concerns.
And this comes on top of the Russian airspace, which complicates
operations. Traﬃc in this pivotal region on the East-West corridor
halved in March and April and was still 15% lower year-on-year in
August 2026. Before the war, the Middle East accounted for 9.5% of
global revenue passenger kilometres (RPKs). On balance, the

setback in the region shaved some 2-3 percentage points oﬀ global
growth with additional impacts elsewhere.
Despite a sharp increase in jet fuel prices, airline response remained
constrained, with most measures limited to cancelling certain
unproﬁtable routes rather than undertaking broader capacity

Jet fuel prices remain elevated, geopolitical and security risks
persist, and aircraft maintenance and delivery constraints continue

to limit growth. Disruptions are also becoming more frequent,
partly due to more extreme weather. In this environment, the
performance gap between airlines is widening.
Yet air travel demand has proved remarkably resilient despite the
turmoil and higher ticket prices. You might think geopolitical and
safety concerns would keep travellers on the ground, but it appears

Jet fuel continues to trade high in Q4 2026
European jet fuel prices in $ per tonne (ARA)
Source: Reﬁnitiv, ING Research
Cost shock hit proﬁtability, but for some
airlines more than others

Jet fuel is often the largest or second-largest cost item for airlines.
In 2025, it accounted for 27% of total operational costs on average.
This share could move towards 35% in 2026 as a result of
persistently high jet fuel prices and elevated crack spreads, caused
by the loss of Middle Eastern supplies and tight global availability.
Further geopolitical developments remain highly uncertain. Even if
the Middle East conﬂict is resolved and oil prices retreat, tight

reﬁnery capacity and depleted inventories are likely to keep middle-
distillate prices, including jet fuel, elevated well into 2027.
High fuel prices impact proﬁts in US and
Europe, low cost carriers most vulnerable

### Page 7

Operational proﬁt (EBIT) in % per peiod
Source: Company reports, ING Research
Fuel hedging provides less cushioning in
2027; fares must respond
Airlines employ very diﬀerent strategies to protect themselves
against fuel price shocks, using derivatives based on crude oil or jet
fuel. European airlines generally hedged 40-90% of their jet fuel
consumption, cushioning the initial impact of the price shock.
However, the Middle East conﬂict has lasted too long to avoid a

signiﬁcant impact. Existing hedges gradually roll oﬀ, increasing
exposure to prevailing market prices. Ryanair, which remains (80%)
hedged until the second quarter of 2027, has already warned of
signiﬁcantly higher fares.
Outside, the picture is mixed, but hedge ratios are generally lower,
and airlines in the US and China, for instance, don’t hedge at all.
This means that two airlines operating similar routes can face very
diﬀerent fuel bills.
Airline tickets in the US responded much

faster to the cost shock than in Europe

Index ticket prices vs consumer price index (CPI, seasonally adjusted) in

Fares already sharply higher in the US, less
so in Europe
The fuel price shock will increasingly feed through into higher fares.
US airlines’ practice of retaining relatively high exposure to fuel price
volatility resulted in an almost immediate pass-through, with ticket
prices rising solidly in the months after jet fuel prices started to rise.

It is also easier to raise prices when domestic competitors face a
similar cost shock.
The situation is diﬀerent in Europe, where hedging provided more
initial protection. This also means fares may still need to rise
signiﬁcantly if fuel prices fail to fall. Coverage gradually expires as
2027 progresses, leaving airlines increasingly exposed to higher
spot prices.
Airline proﬁts hit, but industry remains
proﬁtable

### Page 9

Source: IATA, *ING Research based on IATA
More factors are driving up costs,
particularly in Europe
Jet fuel is the clear outlier this year, but wage and maintenance
costs are also rising faster than the average price level. Borrowing
costs and aircraft leasing rates remain another factor, although the
impact depends on individual airlines’ strategies and ﬁnancial
structures.
European airlines also face rising costs from the European Union
Emissions Trading System (ETS) for ﬂights within the European

Economic Area. In addition, fuel suppliers are required to blend
sustainable aviation fuel (SAF) under ReFuelEU Aviation, starting at
2% and rising to 6% by 2030*. SAF still trades at a signiﬁcant
premium. This puts a price on emissions, and in combination, this
adds some $300 per tonne of jet fuel in 2026 (BNEF, ING Research).
Meanwhile, France and the Netherlands have either raised or plan
to raise ticket taxes, particularly on long-haul ﬂying, while Germany

has scaled its levy back and Sweden has abolished it.
High fuel prices put more pressure on low-
cost carriers
*A revision proposal is in progress for aviation, drawing a circle of 5,000 km around
Frankfurt for application of ETS, which would hit Middle East airlines most as they will
then be in scope. **In 2025, the blend rate stood at 2.8%

### Page 10

Low-cost carriers are particularly exposed because fuel accounts
for a larger share of their cost base, and proﬁt bases are more
vulnerable. High hedge ratios initially cushion volatility, but these
hedges gradually roll oﬀ. The more challenging environment is
already visible in the performance of several European low-cost
carriers and will remain an important theme in 2027 if fuel prices
stay elevated.
Premium carriers have more room to manoeuvre. Demand for

premium seats (business, premium economy) is less price-
sensitive, providing airlines with greater scope to pass on higher
costs. Both United Airlines and Delta Airlines reported little impact
after price increases of 15-20% in the second to third quarters.
Passengers are booking later, but airlines continue to indicate a
strong propensity to ﬂy, especially in premium and premium-
economy classes. A minority also accounts for a large share of
ﬂights. In the Netherlands, for example, 25% of people account for

75% of journeys. These frequent travellers tend to be less price-
sensitive.
There has therefore been some demand destruction wiping out part
of the underlying growth. So far, though, the propensity to travel
has remained resilient.
Aviation remains proﬁtable, but margins hit
a post-pandemic low
The fuel shock has signiﬁcantly aﬀected margins. On average,
proﬁts in the global industry are expected to drop meaningfully
below 5%. Airlines including Ryanair, Southwest Airlines and AirAsia

have scaled back capacity on unproﬁtable routes as elevated fuel
prices persisted longer than expected.
According to global aviation consultancy Cirium, around 1% of
global air travel capacity will be removed heading into the Northern
Hemisphere winter of 2026-27. In less mature markets, maintaining
volumes while passing on the higher fuel bill is more diﬃcult.
Airlines may also prioritise less fuel-eﬃcient, older-generation
aircraft over capacity growth.

### Page 11

Industry headwinds could become a catalyst for further
consolidation, ranging from minority stakes to full takeovers.
Fares could retreat if fuel prices ease, but
don't expect too much
Dynamic and algorithm-based pricing is becoming increasingly
common. This helps airlines optimise already historically high load
factors (84% in 2026 so far) but can also contribute to greater fare
volatility, especially as passengers book later amid uncertainty.
If fuel prices ease in 2027, fares could drop back. Yet relatively

strong demand, constrained aircraft availability and general
inﬂation mean a return to early-2026 fare levels looks unlikely.
Cargo beneﬁts from high-tech demand and
supply-chain volatility
Air cargo continues to perform well as a fast transport option in an
increasingly volatile and less predictable world. Volume growth
remained almost 5% year-to-date, while freight rates remained
attractive for carriers.
While e-commerce drove much of the growth in recent years,

digitalisation and large-scale AI investment increasingly support
shipments of high-value technology products. Strong cargo
performance helped cushion pressure on airline results in 2026.
European carriers also beneﬁted from reduced available freight
capacity from Middle Eastern competitors.
Freighter capacity received a boost during the pandemic through
passenger-to-freighter conversions, but that boost has largely run
its course. New-generation A350 and Boeing 777 freighters should

add capacity in the coming years.
Aircargo volume resilient in disruptive
geopolitical environment

Future demand 2027 and onwards
Aviation’s centre of gravity is increasingly
shifting eastwards
The centre of gravity of global aviation is increasingly shifting
eastwards. Trips per capita are expected to see strong increases
over the next two decades, particularly in Asia. This matters even
more because these markets are far more populous.
Until recently, around 90% of the world’s population did not ﬂy in a
given year, but this is gradually changing. As a result, ﬂeet growth is

expected to be strongest in Asia, with its share of the global aircraft
ﬂeet increasing from around 33% to approximately 45%.
People still expected to ﬂy more, especially
in India
Trips per capita per year in various countries
